# XR control prototypes for graphty

Status: design sketches, 2026-10-09. Nothing here has been built or tried in a headset.

graphty is a graph (network) visualization and analysis product: the graphty app, a React web UI,
around graphty-element, a web component that draws 2D and 3D node-link graphs with Babylon.js and
already opens WebXR sessions. This document collects 23 XR control prototypes: different ways a
person could drive graphty in a VR headset. Each prototype is a complete control scheme, written
down in enough detail to mock up and test.

The prototypes differ only in interactions, controls, menus and how they combine. They do not
differ in environment, scenery, lighting or graphic design, and none of them proposes a visual
language. Two prototypes that look the same in a screenshot can be completely different to use.

## The fixed setup

Every prototype shares the same setup, so that only the controls vary:

- **Full VR, not AR.** A solid colored background, with no room, scenery or passthrough.
- **The graph floats at chest height about 70-80 cm in front of you.** Prototypes may move it
  (turn, scale, bring it closer) but always start it there.
- **A head-mounted display:** Meta Quest, Samsung Galaxy XR or Apple Vision Pro. Hands are
  available on all three, controllers where the device has them, eye gaze where the device exposes
  it to the app, and voice is optional.
- **Object-first.** graphty works on objects -- the graph, a node, an edge, a group or selection,
  an algorithm run and its result, a style layer, a filter step, a note, the project -- and each
  object has its own verbs. Every prototype has to answer how you get from an object to its verbs.

## What varies: the layers

A prototype is defined by its choices on six layers:

- **Mode model:** how the system knows what your next input means (select then command, holding a
  tool, a held hand sign, drawing a glyph, distance from the graph, and so on).
- **Navigation:** how you turn, move and zoom the graph, or reach a part of it.
- **Selection:** how you pick nodes, edges and sets of them.
- **Menus and command surfaces:** where verbs live and how you find them.
- **Parameter entry:** how you give a number, a threshold or a choice.
- **Feedback:** how you see what will happen and what did happen.

Undo is tracked separately in the tables, because the prototypes differ most there.

## The basic journey

Every prototype walks the same task, on the Florentine families network (marriages between
Renaissance Florentine families, 15 nodes):

1. Enter VR and get oriented.
2. Find the Medici.
3. See who they married into (their neighbors).
4. Find who matters most: run PageRank.
5. Read the result.
6. Color and size the families by PageRank.
7. Keep only the strong families (a filter).
8. Write a note.
9. Undo a mistake.
10. Save.

Advanced journeys follow: a compound rule, algorithm options, comparing two runs, style layers on
subsets, recipes and joining tables. Each prototype also says how it scales to the 60+ algorithm
catalog, to new object types and to plugins.

## Prototypes 1-11

The first eleven prototypes were designed earlier. Each is summarized here in one paragraph; the
new prototypes after them are given in full.

**1. Palette Hand.** The off hand holds a palm-up palette with four faces (Selection, Analyze,
Paint, Steps), flipped by turning the wrist, while the dominant hand pokes at it. You point and
pinch to select, then command from the palette; values are sliders on the palette, cards can be
torn off it and left in the scene, and undo is a palette button. Two hands grab the graph to move
it.

**2. Point and Say.** A fixed spoken grammar, with no AI: you point at something, hold an off-hand
pinch and speak, and "this" binds to whatever you point at. The sentence appears as editable chips
on a command bar, and "go" runs it. Numbers are spoken or set on a chip dial, "undo" undoes, and
you move the graph by grabbing it or by voice.

**3. Gesture Grammar.** Marking menus: pinch, hold and flick in one of eight directions, two
levels deep. Pinching a node and pulling away sets how many hops of neighbors to take; a wrist
twist works as a dial; off-hand thumb micro-gestures confirm and undo. Selection is pinch and
lasso, and two hands grab the graph.

**4. Tool Belt.** Built for controllers. Eight tools sit in belt slots, and algorithms are
cartridges in a rack. Each tool's settings are on a thumbstick radial menu and dial, and trigger
pressure sets strength. You select by painting and by "more like this"; the graph frames itself
and turns on the stick; B undoes, and holding B while moving the stick scrubs back through
history.

**5. Workbench.** A Figma-like dock in VR: toolbar, outline, inspector and a command line, used
with a Bluetooth keyboard. You edit properties rather than issue commands, scrub values by
dragging, select by typed query or in the outline, and steer the view with a world in miniature.
Ctrl+Z and a history list undo.

**6. Workshop.** No menus at all. A pegboard holds physical tools (a scale, a sieve, a spray can,
a pump, a stretch cord, tongs, a magnifier). You pull attribute tags off nodes and load them into
tools, set values with knobs and pulls, and pick nodes with tongs into a tray. Rails hold style
layer sheets, sieves (filters) and history receipts; pulling a receipt off the rail undoes that
step.

**7. Hand Signs.** Static held hand poses set the mode: the off hand makes a sign (a V to
explore, horns to analyze, an L to filter, and so on) and the dominant hand points and fires to
apply. Numbers are finger counts and values come from rolling a fist; a thumbs down undoes, and
fists move the graph.

**8. Grip and Tool.** Shape your hand as if holding a tool (a spray can, scissors, a pen, a claw,
a scoop, calipers, a lens) and that tool appears in it. The off hand holds the graph's stand and
turns knobs on it; grip depth and knobs set values; a claw and a scoop select. Flicking a history
strip undoes.

**9. Spellcasting.** Draw a circle in the air (its size and position set the scope), trace a
glyph inside it (a fork, a spiral, a wave, lightning, a brushstroke, a square portal), and close
your fist to cast. Runes orbit the circle for choices, pulling hands apart or pushing sets values,
and turning a hand counterclockwise winds time back. Two palms orbit the graph.

**10. HUD.** A heads-up display that follows the view lazily. A reticle aimed with the head or eyes
locks onto targets, and a pinch with a resting hand acts. Strips at the edges of the view hold the
locked targets, context commands, a results feed and status; a radar and look-and-pull navigate;
values are indirect pinch-drag gauges; an undo counter steps back.

**11. Context Menus.** Right-click menus everywhere. The A button opens a menu at whatever you
clicked (a node, an edge, a selection, a result, a layer or the background), with submenus,
search, inline sliders and steppers; a dock folds out for details. Trigger and lasso select,
grips and the stick move the graph, and the background menu and a history list undo.

## Prototype 12: Lean In

Distance is the mode: how close your hand is to the graph, a cluster or a node decides which verbs exist, and handles grow on that object at the scale you are working at.

### How it differs, layer by layer

| Layer                   | Lean In                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Closest existing prototypes                                                                                              | How it differs                                                                                                                                         |
| ----------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Mode model              | Proximity scoping in three bands: hand near the graph (graph verbs), hand inside the graph (verbs of the nearest pinned set), fingertip at an element (element verbs). No mode switch, no separate select step. The object in scope is always outlined before you act.                                                                  | Palette Hand (1), Context Menus (11): select, then command. Tool Belt (4), Grip and Tool (8): a held tool sets the mode. | Nothing is chosen to enter a mode. Where your hand is IS the mode, and the scope (graph, set, node) comes with it.                                     |
| Navigation              | Your body: step and lean. A fist in the graph band (either hand) reels the box toward you along your head direction, or sends it back, with detents at home (75 cm) and arm's reach (50 cm). A ring at the box's base turns it; a Zoom arrow beside it magnifies the graph inside the box; a set's Fit tab fills the box with that set. | Palette Hand (1), Gesture Grammar (3): two-hand grab. Workbench (5): miniature. Tool Belt (4): auto-frame.               | You never grab the graph. Getting closer is literal, and getting closer is also how you change scope. Magnifying is a handle, not a gesture.           |
| Selection               | Hover is selection. A deliberate fingertip poke pins an element; an open palm held face down inside the box gathers what lies under it, and closing the palm pins the gathered set.                                                                                                                                                     | Palette Hand (1): point and pinch. Context Menus (11): trigger and lasso. Grip and Tool (8): claw and scoop.             | No pointing ray and no lasso. The thing your hand is near is already selected; pinning only makes it stay.                                             |
| Menus / command surface | Handles on the object itself, at the scale you are working at: the graph's bounding box, a set's hull, a node, a result's ladder, a layer slat, a filter blade. Six handle shapes, each with one meaning.                                                                                                                               | Gesture Grammar (3): marking menus. Context Menus (11): menus at the object. Palette Hand (1): a hand-held palette.      | There is no menu, panel or palette. Every verb is a handle you drag, and the handles on offer change as your hand moves in.                            |
| Parameters              | Dragging a handle is the verb and its value together, with detents. Releasing commits; dragging back past the rest notch cancels. Moving slowly gives fine values (the gain falls with hand speed, like pointer acceleration).                                                                                                          | Palette Hand (1): sliders. Tool Belt (4): stick dial. Gesture Grammar (3): wrist twist.                                  | There is no separate value step: the drag that chooses "neighbors" also says how many hops, live, before you let go.                                   |
| Feedback                | Handles appear and resize as the hand crosses each band; the object in scope is outlined; every handle shows its value and how many elements it changes. Detents click (and pulse on controllers).                                                                                                                                      | HUD (10): edge strips. Context Menus (11): menu highlight.                                                               | Feedback sits on the handle you are holding, and the count of what will change is always visible before release.                                       |
| Undo                    | A "stop" palm pushed away. Inside the graph it undoes the last change made from the outlined object's handles; from the graph band or a lowered hand it undoes globally.                                                                                                                                                                | Palette Hand (1): a button. Hand Signs (7): thumbs down. Grip and Tool (8): history strip.                               | Undo has a target. An old mistake on one node can be undone without unwinding everything done since, and global undo is reachable without reaching in. |

**Setup.** Fixed environment: solid colored background in full VR, the graph floating at chest height about 75 cm away inside a faint bounding box about 45 cm across. Devices: Meta Quest 3 / 3S, Samsung Galaxy XR, Apple Vision Pro. Primary input is tracked hands (fingertip position, pinch, open palm, fist). Controllers work as a stand-in hand on Quest and Galaxy XR. Eyes are not used. Voice is optional and only types text into an open text field (a note card or the Find field). Every input works with either hand, so the whole scheme can be used one-handed and seated. Band sizes and hysteresis are settings, so a user with a tremor can widen them.

### The control vocabulary

| Input                                                                   | Meaning, everywhere                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Hand distance from the graph                                            | Which band you are in, and so which object is in scope: hand below the box's base or more than 30 cm from the box = rest (no handles); within 30 cm of the box, outside it = graph; inside the box = neighborhood (the nearest pinned set); fingertip within 3 cm of a node, edge, rung, slat, blade or flag = that element. The object in scope is outlined.                                                 |
| Fingertip hover                                                         | Selects. The nearest element swells and its handles grow.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Fingertip poke                                                          | Pins what you poked, or unpins it if it was pinned. A poke is a quick in-and-back (under 0.4 s) toward the element; a fingertip sliding past or through elements on its way somewhere is never a poke. Poking a set's hull surface, away from its nodes, unpins the set. A pinned node keeps its handles; a pinned readout stays open. Pins are view state, not changes, so they are not in the undo history. |
| Open palm held face down inside the box                                 | Gathers what lies in a ball just under the palm. Spreading the fingers widens the ball (about 3 to 15 cm); the palm shows the count it holds.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Closing that palm to a fist                                             | Pins the gathered set; it becomes a set with a hull and its own handles. Opening the palm or lifting it out of the box instead lets the gather go.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Pinch on a handle and drag                                              | Does the handle's verb, with the value given by how far you drag. Scope is locked to the handle's object from pinch to release.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Release the pinch                                                       | Commits.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Drag back past the rest notch, then release                             | Cancels; nothing changes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Drag speed                                                              | Fine control on continuous values: the slower the hand, the smaller the change per centimeter. List detents (names, algorithms) keep a fixed 2.5 cm spacing so they can be learned by reach.                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| "Stop" palm (fingers up, palm facing away) pushed away, short and quick | Undo. Inside the box it undoes the last change made from the outlined object's handles; from the graph band, or with the hand lowered, it undoes the latest change globally.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Pinch a ghost handle                                                    | Redo: puts back what the last undo took away. The ghost stays until your next change; the readout plate always carries a ghost Redo tab for the global history.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Fist in the graph band (either hand), pulled toward you / pushed away   | Reels the box along your head direction / sends it back, with detents at home (75 cm) and arm's reach (50 cm). A fist without motion does nothing; a fist inside the box only closes a gather.                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Step, lean, crouch                                                      | Moves you; the graph stays put.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Voice (optional)                                                        | Types into an open text field (a note card or the Find field). Nothing else.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

The two open-palm gestures cannot be mistaken for each other: gather needs the palm facing down (within 45 degrees) and held still inside the box; undo needs the palm facing away from you (within 35 degrees), fingers up, a push faster than about 0.8 m/s, and is ignored for half a second after any handle release. No gesture uses a palm turned toward your face, which Quest and Vision Pro reserve for their system menus.

### The handle kit

Every verb on every object is one of six handle shapes. The shape tells you how to drag it and what kind of thing it does.

| Shape  | How you drag it                                                                                           | Always means                                                             | Examples                                                                                         |
| ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Arrow  | Pull along its axis through detents; a list arrow bends once, up for a group and sideways for its members | An amount, or a place in a list; release commits that value              | hops of neighbors, size range, zoom, Find (letters, then names), Run (families, then algorithms) |
| Ring   | Turn it through detents                                                                                   | Choose one of a few                                                      | color palette, layer order, turntable angle                                                      |
| Blade  | Slide a plane along a scale                                                                               | Keep what is on one side (a filter step)                                 | "PageRank at least 0.070"                                                                        |
| Tab    | Pull past its single detent and release                                                                   | A one-shot verb; its readout says what it will change before you release | Hide, Group, Fit, Remove, Save, Save as recipe                                                   |
| Flag   | Pull it out of its socket; push it back to close                                                          | A note on this object                                                    | node note, graph note                                                                            |
| Thread | Pull from this object to another; drop on empty space to cancel                                           | A verb between two objects                                               | shortest path, compare two runs                                                                  |

### What each band offers

| Band         | Where your hand is                                                   | Object in scope                                                                                                       | Handles that grow                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Rest         | below the box's base, or more than 30 cm away                        | none                                                                                                                  | none; the box outline, its readout plate and the ghost Redo tab only                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Graph        | within 30 cm of the box, outside it (enter at 30 cm, leave at 35 cm) | the whole graph and project                                                                                           | Run arrow (top), Find arrow (top left), note flag (top corner), readout plate, Save tab and Save as recipe tab (front edge), turntable ring and Zoom arrow (base), layer slats (left side), result ladders and filter blades (right side)                                                                                          |
| Neighborhood | inside the box                                                       | the pinned set whose hull is nearest your hand; with no pinned set, nothing but labels under your hand and the gather | hull handles: Grow arrow (hops), Run arrow (on this set only), Size arrow and Color ring (a style layer on this set), Blade (a filter inside this set), Group tab, Hide tab, Fit tab, note flag                                                                                                                                    |
| Element      | fingertip within 3 cm (leave at 5 cm)                                | one node, edge, ladder rung or name plate, layer slat, blade, or note flag                                            | node: Neighbors arrow, Path thread, Hide tab, note flag; edge: Hide tab, note flag; ladder: Size arrow, Color ring, Blade; ladder name plate: option handles, Compare thread, Remove tab; slat: the Size arrow or Color ring that made it (to edit it), order ring, Hide tab, Remove tab; blade: Remove tab; note card: Delete tab |

Once a pinch grabs a handle the scope is locked until release, so reaching across the graph to a handle never changes what it acts on. Handles grow on the side of the object facing you, offset from the fingertip so the hand does not cover them, and only the object in scope and pinned objects show handles (at most nine on a set's hull, spread around it).

### The journey

1. **Enter.**
    - **What you do:** On graphty's 2D page, open the Florentine families sample and choose Enter VR. Put the headset on and stand, or sit, facing the graph.
    - **What you see:** The 15 families floating at chest height inside a faint box. Your hands are down, so nothing else shows: no menus, no handles. The readout plate on the box's front edge reads "Florentine families".
    - **Controls:** none; rest band.

2. **Get oriented.**
    - **What you do:** Raise a hand toward the box, stopping short of it. Poke the readout plate to pin it open. Lean left and right to see the graph's depth. If seated, make a fist near the box and pull it to the arm's-reach detent, then turn the base ring a detent or two.
    - **What you see:** As your hand crosses into the graph band, the box outline brightens and its handles grow along its edges. The pinned readout lists "15 families, 20 marriages, 1 piece, undirected" and the node attributes the sample carries. Labels are shown for the largest nodes only. A reeled box clicks into its arm's-reach detent, close enough that its far side is within reach.
    - **Controls:** hand distance (graph band), fingertip poke (pin the readout), body lean, fist reel, turntable ring.

3. **Find the Medici.**
    - **What you do:** Move your hand into the box. Labels bloom on the nodes near your hand. Slide toward the node labeled Medici until your fingertip is beside it, and poke it. (In a graph too big to scan, you would pull the Find arrow at graph band up through letters and sideways through names and release on "Medici", or pinch its field and say or type "Medici"; either pins it.)
    - **What you see:** Labels appear on the region under your hand as you enter the box. Medici swells as your fingertip nears it, gets an outline, and its handles grow: a Neighbors arrow, a Path thread, a Hide tab, a note flag. A readout beside it says "Medici, 6 ties". The poke pins it, so its handles stay when your hand moves.
    - **Controls:** hand distance (neighborhood, then element band), fingertip hover, fingertip poke.

4. **Who they married into.**
    - **What you do:** Pinch Medici's Neighbors arrow and pull it outward to the first detent. Release.
    - **What you see:** While you pull, the six neighbors light up and the arrow's tip reads "1 hop: 6 families, 7 marriages among them". Pulling on to the second detent would read "2 hops: 11 families"; you stop at one. On release the six (Acciaiuoli, Albizzi, Barbadori, Ridolfi, Salviati, Tornabuoni) and Medici become a pinned set with a soft hull around them, and the hull carries its own handles.
    - **Controls:** pinch and drag an arrow, detent, release to commit.

5. **Who matters most (PageRank).**
    - **What you do:** Pull your hand back out of the box to the graph band. Pinch the Run arrow on top of the box and pull it up: the algorithm families pass as detents (Centrality, Community, Paths, ...). Stop at Centrality, slide sideways through its members to PageRank, and release.
    - **What you see:** The arrow's readout follows the pull: "Centrality", then "PageRank on 15 families". On release, PageRank runs on the whole graph (the graph band means graph scope, even though a set is pinned) and a result ladder stands up beside the box's right side: one rung per family, ranked, with the run's name plate on top.
    - **Controls:** hand distance (graph band), pinch and drag a list arrow along its two legs, release to run.

6. **Read the result.**
    - **What you do:** Bring your hand to the ladder. Run your fingertip down its rungs.
    - **What you see:** From the graph band the ladder shows the top three names. As your fingertip nears it, the rungs spread and show values: Medici 0.146, Guadagni 0.098, Strozzi 0.088, Albizzi 0.079, Tornabuoni 0.071, and so on down to Acciaiuoli 0.031. The rung under your fingertip lights its family in the graph (hover is selection, here too).
    - **Controls:** hand distance (element band on the ladder), fingertip hover.

7. **Color and size by PageRank.**
    - **What you do:** Near the ladder, pinch its Size arrow and pull it out until the largest family is about three times the smallest; release. Then pinch its Color ring and turn it two detents to the sequential palette; release.
    - **What you see:** Node sizes follow the pull live; the arrow reads "Size by PageRank, 1x to 3x, 15 families". The ring's readout names each palette as it passes and recolors the graph live. On release, a key appears on the box ("Size: PageRank", "Color: PageRank") and a new layer slat appears on the box's left side.
    - **Controls:** pinch and drag an arrow, pinch and turn a ring, release to commit.

8. **Keep only strong families (filter).**
    - **What you do:** Pinch the Blade at the top of the ladder and slide it down the rungs to the detent at 0.070. Release.
    - **What you see:** Rungs below the blade dim, and those families fade out of the graph as you slide. The blade reads "PageRank at least 0.070: keeps 5 of 15 families, 4 marriages". After release, Medici, Guadagni, Strozzi, Albizzi and Tornabuoni remain, and the box's readout says "Showing 5 of 15 -- 1 filter". The blade stays where you left it, as the filter step's own handle.
    - **Controls:** pinch and slide a blade, detents, release to commit.

9. **Write a note.**
    - **What you do:** Lean in to Medici until your fingertip is beside it, pinch its note flag and pull it out. Dictate "Married into six families; the hub of the network", or type it on the small keyboard that unfolds under the card. Push the flag back into its socket to close it.
    - **What you see:** A note card on a short stalk from Medici, with your text. Closed, it becomes a small flag marker on the node.
    - **Controls:** hand distance (element band), pinch and pull a flag, voice (optional) or in-scene keyboard.

10. **Undo a mistake.**
    - **What you do:** You reach back to the note flag to fix a word, but your pinch lands on the Hide tab beside it; you pull, glance past the readout "Hide Medici: 1 family, 4 marriages hidden", and release. Medici vanishes. Keep your hand where Medici was and push a "stop" palm away, short and quick.
    - **What you see:** A hidden node leaves a ghost outline until your next change, so the spot is still there to reach and it is outlined as the undo's target. The push undoes Medici's last change only: Medici reappears with its note intact, and a ghost Hide tab hangs beside it (pinch it to redo). Here a push from the graph band would have done the same, because the hide was the latest change. The difference shows when the mistake is older: had you also written the graph note since, the push at Medici would still restore Medici without touching the graph note.
    - **Controls:** "stop" palm pushed away (scoped undo), ghost handle (redo).

11. **Save.**
    - **What you do:** Pull back to the graph band. Pinch the Save tab on the box's front edge, pull it down past its detent, and release.
    - **What you see:** The tab reads "Save project" while pulled; on release the readout says "Saved Florentine families". The Save as recipe tab beside it is not used here.
    - **Controls:** hand distance (graph band), pinch and pull a tab, release to commit.

### Advanced journeys

- **Compound rule.** Blades stack, and each one is a filter step: a blade on the PageRank ladder plus a blade on a Degree ladder means both must hold (AND). For "strong families among the Medici in-laws", leave the in-law set pinned and use the Blade on the set's hull instead of the graph's ladder: it filters inside that set only. OR is made by gathering both results' sets under one palm and closing it (a union), which is honest about being a set, not a saved rule. Saved rules with OR and NOT are weak in this scheme. A blade's Remove tab takes its filter step out, with the count it will bring back shown before release.
- **Tuning algorithm options.** Bring the fingertip to the ladder's name plate (the run's object). Its options grow as arrows and rings: a damping arrow at 0.85 with detents every 0.05, an iterations arrow, a "weighted" ring. Dragging an option reruns live as a preview, with the ladder's rungs shifting and a count "3 families change rank". Release commits a new run beside the old one; drag back past rest to keep the original.
- **Comparing two runs.** Pull the Compare thread from one ladder's name plate to the other ladder. The two ladders join rung to rung by family, so crossing lines show who moved, and the thread's readout says how many changed rank and by how much. Drop the thread on empty space to cancel.
- **Layers on subsets.** Gather a region with the palm and close it to pin the set; its hull's Size arrow and Color ring make a style layer that paints only that set. Each layer is a slat on the box's left side; fingertip on a slat grows the handle that made it (drag it again to edit the layer), its order ring (turn to move it up or down the stack), its Hide tab and its Remove tab.
- **Removing a run.** The name plate's Remove tab reads, before release, what goes with it: "Remove PageRank: also removes 1 style layer and 1 filter step that use it".
- **Dense or large graphs.** Gather a rough region with the palm (it needs no precise touch), close it, and pull the hull's Fit tab: the graph magnifies inside the box until that set fills it, and what falls outside the box is clipped. The base's Zoom arrow dragged back to 1x returns to the whole graph. Navigation and zoom are view state, not in the undo history.
- **Recipes.** The Save as recipe tab saves the runs, style layers and filter steps without the data. On another graph, the recipes appear as one more family on the Run arrow's pull; releasing on one replays it, with a count of what it changed. Editing a recipe step by step stays on the 2D page.
- **Joining tables.** Stays on the 2D page. Handles act on what is drawn; choosing files and matching columns is reading and typing, which this scheme has no good handle for.

### How it scales

- **The 60+ algorithm catalog.** The Run arrow is a two-leg list: up through about ten families, then sideways through each family's members, every one a detent with its name, at a fixed 2.5 cm spacing so the reach to a favorite is learnable. The longest pull is about 25 cm each way. The readout always names the detent under your hand, so nothing must be memorized. Past about 120 entries the pull gets too long; the Run arrow would then need the same text field Find has (dictate or type a name), which this design leaves for later.
- **New object types.** Each object type declares its handle set from the six shapes and the band it lives in. A new type (a path, a time slice, a group of groups) brings handles, never a panel, and users already know how each shape drags.
- **Plugins.** A plugin algorithm appears as a detent in its family on the Run arrow and gets an options plate built from its option list (numbers become arrows, choices become rings, flags become tabs). A plugin object type ships its handle set.
- **The limit.** Density. The element band needs nodes farther apart than the fingertip's 3 cm radius and the tracking jitter (about 1 cm with hands). Reeling the box closer does not help, because it moves the box without spreading the nodes. A few hundred nodes at 45 cm is fine; beyond that you magnify with the Zoom arrow or a set's Fit tab, so node spacing in the box grows. Handle crowding is capped: only the object in scope and pinned objects show handles, at most about nine on a hull and four on an element.

### On each device

| Device                                     | Inputs it exposes to WebXR                                                                                                                       | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Meta Quest 3 / 3S, hands                   | Hand input with 25 joints per hand; no eye tracking on the web                                                                                   | The whole scheme runs here, computed from joints (fingertip, pinch distance, palm normal, finger curl). No haptics with bare hands, so detents are a click sound and a visible tick. Fingertip jitter sets the 3 cm element band. Whether the browser's speech recognition works inside an immersive session must be checked; without it, notes use the in-scene keyboard.                                                                                                                                             |
| Meta Quest 3 / 3S, controllers             | Touch controllers: pose, trigger, grip, buttons, haptics                                                                                         | A point 4 cm ahead of the controller is the fingertip. Trigger = pinch. A quick poke of that point = poke. Inside the box, hold grip = open palm gathering (the thumbstick widens or narrows the ball); pull the trigger while gripping = close and pin; let go of grip alone = drop the gather. In the graph band, grip and pull = reel. B or Y = undo, with the same scope rule as the palm push. Detents pulse.                                                                                                     |
| Samsung Galaxy XR, hands                   | WebXR hand input in Chrome on Android XR                                                                                                         | Same as Quest hands, to be confirmed on the device. The scheme does not use eye gaze, which we have not confirmed Chrome exposes to WebXR.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Samsung Galaxy XR, controllers             | The optional controllers, as WebXR gamepads                                                                                                      | Same mapping as Quest controllers, if they are paired and report a grip button and haptics.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Apple Vision Pro, eyes and hands           | Safari sends a transient pointer (gaze plus pinch, with the gaze ray revealed only when the pinch starts) and, with permission, full hand joints | The scheme runs on hand joints and needs that permission; if it is refused there is no fingertip to measure distance from, so graphty must offer a different control scheme there. It ignores the gaze pointer, because every pinch also arrives as a pointer select; the app must drop that event whenever the hand joints show the pinch is on a handle. The system's eye-hover effect is not available to web content, which is why hover here is the fingertip's, not the eyes'. No haptics: sound and ticks only. |
| Apple Vision Pro, PS VR2 Sense controllers | visionOS supports the controllers; whether Safari exposes them as WebXR gamepads must be checked on the device                                   | If exposed, the Quest controller mapping applies. If not, hands only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

### Why it should work

- Physical navigation helps people read large displays: in Ball, North and Bowman's "Move to improve" (CHI 2007), people who walked up to a large display found and compared targets faster than people who panned and zoomed. Here stepping in is both the zoom and the scope change.
- Semantic zoom (Pad++, Bederson and Hollan, 1994) showed that changing what is shown, not just its size, with distance keeps detail out of the way until it is wanted. Lean In applies it to verbs.
- Proxemic interaction (Greenberg, Marquardt and colleagues, 2011) uses distance as implicit input so that people do not have to switch modes by hand; their proxemic media player changed its controls as the viewer approached.
- Gizmos in Blender, Maya and Unity have taught millions of people that a handle on the object means "drag this to change that property", with the value visible while dragging. Direct manipulation (Shneiderman, 1983): continuous feedback and reversible actions.
- Hover before touch is shipped: visionOS shows a hover state before a direct touch, and Meta's poke interactions show hover as the fingertip approaches a button. People already expect things to react as a finger nears them.
- Drag back to cancel is shipped and familiar: pull-to-refresh, swipe actions on phones, and the "drag off the button to cancel" rule of desktop buttons.
- Speed-dependent gain is how every desktop pointer already gets both reach and precision from one hand (Casiez and colleagues, 2008, on control-display gain), so fine control needs no extra gesture.
- Selective and regional undo are studied: Berlage's selective undo (1994) and Seifried and colleagues' regional undo for large surfaces (CHI 2012) found people can understand undoing one region's changes without the global history.

### What it would teach us

- Do people experience distance bands as zoom (natural) or as hidden modes (mode errors)? Can they predict which verbs they will find before they reach in?
- What band sizes and hysteresis keep handles stable under real hand tracking on each device, and does a 3 cm element band work at 15, 77 and 300 nodes?
- Is hover-is-selection a Midas touch in 3D: does scope jump while people reach for a handle, even with scope locked on pinch, and does the poke rule stop accidental pins as a hand passes through the graph?
- Can handles alone carry a 60+ algorithm catalog, or does the two-leg Run pull break down without search?
- Do people understand scoped undo, and do they prefer it to a single global undo once they have made an older mistake?
- How tiring is it? How long can people work with a hand raised near the graph, and how much do the arm's-reach detent and seated turntable help?
- How many handle drags does the basic journey take compared with the desktop app's clicks?

### Known risks

- **Arm fatigue.** Every verb needs a raised hand. Mitigation: graph-band handles sit on the box's lower edge; the arm's-reach detent brings the box close, and reeling while looking down brings it lower; rest band shows nothing, so lowering a hand is the natural pause, and global undo still works from there.
- **Seated reach.** At 75 cm the far half of the box is out of a seated arm's reach. Mitigation: the arm's-reach detent at 50 cm, offered on the readout plate's first open when the headset reports a seated height.
- **Accidental scope changes.** A drifting hand crosses a band and the verbs change under it. Mitigation: hysteresis on every band edge (and band sizes as a setting), the outline that always shows the object in scope, scope locked while a pinch holds a handle, and pinning for anything you want to keep.
- **Accidental pins.** A hand moving through the box touches nodes on the way. Mitigation: only a quick in-and-back poke toward an element pins it; sliding past or through does not.
- **Scoped undo with dependents.** Undoing an old change on one object may break later changes built on it (a style layer on a run). Mitigation: the undo previews the later changes it would also undo, by count, and needs a second push within two seconds to go ahead.
- **Occlusion.** Your hand covers the node you are working on. Mitigation: handles grow outward on the side facing you and offset from the fingertip; readouts float above the hand.
- **No haptics on hands.** Detents can be felt only with controllers. Mitigation: a click sound and a tick mark at every detent, and a readout that names the value.
- **False undo.** A quick move after a drag reads as a push-away. Mitigation: require a palm facing away with fingers up and a minimum speed, ignore pushes within half a second of releasing a handle, and leave a ghost handle so redo is one pinch.
- **Handle crowding on a set.** A hull carries up to nine handles. Mitigation: they spread around the hull, and those on the far side grow as you move your hand around it.
- **Dense graphs.** The element band fails when nodes are closer than the tracking jitter. Mitigation: magnify with the Zoom arrow or a set's Fit tab; the readout says when nodes are too close to pick one by touch.
- **Text entry.** Notes need typing, and an in-scene keyboard is slow. Mitigation: optional dictation where the browser supports it; short notes; long writing stays on the 2D page.
- **Gaze pinch on Vision Pro.** Safari reports every pinch as a pointer select too, and without hand permission the scheme cannot run. Mitigation: filter those events whenever hand joints are on a handle, ask for hand permission at Enter VR with the reason, and test on the device early.

### Comparison row

| #   | Name    | One-line idea                                                                                                                                         | Mode model                                                       | Navigation                                                                                                                              | Selection                                                                          | Command surface                                                                                         | Parameters                                                                                                    | Undo                                                                                                           |
| --- | ------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 12  | Lean In | Distance is the mode: hand distance to the graph, a cluster or a node decides which verbs exist; handles grow on that object at the scale you work at | proximity scoping in three bands, scope outlined, no select step | step and lean; fist in the graph band reels the box (home and arm's-reach detents); base ring turns, Zoom arrow and set Fit tab magnify | hover is selection; deliberate poke pins; palm over a region gathers, closing pins | handles (arrow, ring, blade, tab, flag, thread) on the graph box, set hull, node, ladder, slat or blade | dragging a handle is verb and value, with detents; slow drag is fine; release commits; back past rest cancels | "stop" palm push: scoped to the outlined object inside the graph, global from the graph band or a lowered hand |

## Prototype 13: Hand of Cards

A fan of cards in the off hand, each a ready-to-run verb with frozen settings; play a card onto a target and it stays on that object as the record of what was done to it, and lifting it off undoes just that.

### How it differs, layer by layer

| Layer                    | Hand of Cards                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Closest existing prototypes, and the difference                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Mode model               | Object to deal, verb to play. Pinching an object makes it your working object and deals its verbs as cards, each with its settings already decided; you then pick a card and choose the target last by dropping it there (any valid target, not only the working object). The played card stays on its target as the record of what was done to it. | Palette Hand (1), Point and Say (2), Gesture Grammar (3) and Context Menus (11) select, then command; Tool Belt (4), Workshop (6) and Grip and Tool (8) hold a tool and keep applying it. A card is used once, it is aimed at its target like a tool but carries its own settings, and its record stays on the object, not in a history list. |
| Navigation               | Push the rim of the graph's base with an open hand to turn it like a lazy Susan; two open hands on the rim pulled apart or together scale it. A card that names a specific node (a search result, a selection) turns the graph until that node faces you.                                                                                           | Two-hand grab (1, 3), auto-frame plus stick turntable (4), miniature (5), radar (10) and grips (11). Here the turntable is touched directly, and a card that knows its target brings the target to you, so finding and aiming are one motion.                                                                                                 |
| Selection                | Pinching nodes toggles them in a selection card in your hand; the selection is itself a card that can be played onto a verb card (to scope it) or onto the graph (to keep it as a group).                                                                                                                                                           | Point and pinch (1), lasso (3), painting (4), tongs into a tray (6) and lock-on (10) make a selection that lives in the scene. Here the selection is a thing in your hand that is played like any other card.                                                                                                                                 |
| Menus / command surfaces | A fan of cards dealt by the type of your working object, with three fixed slots (selection, Note, Copy); at the fan's ends a draw pile that searches the full catalog, a discard pile, and a "last play" chip. The fan rides on the off palm or rests on a ledge in front of you. Plugins ship as new cards.                                        | Palette Hand (1) has fixed faces you flip; Context Menus (11) open at the object; Workshop (6) has a fixed pegboard. Here the surface re-deals itself, and every command is a single object you carry and place.                                                                                                                              |
| Parameter entry          | No dials and no sliders. You change settings only by playing modifier cards (damping 0.7, top 10, at least 0.07) onto a played card, or by choosing which tuned copy of a card to play. Exact values are minted by typing them in the draw pile. A tuned set can be copied into one recipe card.                                                    | Every designed prototype has a continuous control: sliders (1), wrist twist (3), stick dial and trigger pressure (4), scrubbing (5), knobs (6, 8), pull distance (9), gauges (10), in-menu sliders (11). Here every setting is a discrete, readable card.                                                                                     |
| Feedback                 | A card held over a target previews its effect as a ghost before you commit; targets the card cannot act on do not light; played cards stay on their objects with their outcome on their face. Pinching a played card lights everything that would change if you lifted it.                                                                          | Feedback elsewhere arrives after the command (result feeds in 10, receipts on rails in 6). Here you see the effect before you commit, on the real graph, and the record of every action stays on the object it changed.                                                                                                                       |
| Undo                     | Lift a played card off its object to remove just its effect. It comes back to your hand carrying the cards played onto it, so playing it again restores them all. The "last play" chip lifts the most recent play wherever it sits.                                                                                                                 | Undo in the designed prototypes is linear: a button (1, 4), a word (2), a thumb swipe (3), a history list (5, 11), winding time (9). Here undo is per object and out of order: you can remove the third thing you did and keep the fourth.                                                                                                    |

**Setup.** Fixed environment: solid colored background in full VR, the graph floating at chest height about 75 cm away, sitting on a thin round base about 45 cm across whose front edge is about 50 cm from you, inside easy reach. The front edge carries the graph's name plate and the row of cards played onto the graph. Just below and in front of the base, about 40 cm from you, is a ledge where the fan rests when it is not on your hand. Primary input is bare hands (thumb-index pinch, open palm, index poke, palm orientation). Controllers work where the device has them (trigger for pinch, grip on the rim, buzz on each step). Voice is optional and used only to search the draw pile or dictate a note. Eyes are not used, except through Apple Vision Pro's system look-and-pinch.

### The control vocabulary

| Input (hands)                                                                           | Input (controllers)                          | Meaning, everywhere                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Off hand palm up                                                                        | Off controller held up (the fan rides on it) | Show the fan on your hand. Palm down puts it on the ledge, where it stays usable; in one-handed mode it lives there.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Pinch with the dominant hand                                                            | Trigger                                      | **Take.** On a card in the fan: take it into your hand. On a node or edge: toggle it in your selection card and make it your working object. On the graph's name plate: make the graph your working object. On a played card: pinch to see what lifting it would change; pull it 4 cm away to lift it (undo its effect); release before that and nothing changes. On the draw pile: open search. On the discard pile: take back the last card discarded. On the "last play" chip: same as pinching the last played card. |
| Move a held card                                                                        | Move the controller with a card held         | **Aim.** Valid targets light (with a ring, not color alone). The target nearest the card's top edge shows a ghost of the effect; after the card has stayed on it for about 0.3 s, the card's border fills to show the play is armed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Release the pinch                                                                       | Release the trigger                          | **Play** onto the armed target. Released over nothing, or before the border filled, the card goes back to your hand and nothing happens. Released over the discard pile, it leaves your hand.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Open hand pushes the base rim sideways                                                  | Grip on the rim, move sideways               | Turn the graph about its vertical axis. It stops when the hand leaves; no spin-on.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Both open hands on the rim, pulled apart or together                                    | Both grips on the rim, apart or together     | Scale the graph.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Dominant fingertip drags along the fan                                                  | Off stick left or right                      | Scroll the fan when it holds more cards than fit.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Open dominant hand held near any card, about 10 cm (in the fan, on the ledge or played) | Controller tip near it, no trigger           | Open its face full size, turned toward you: its settings, its result, its note. Changes nothing. A card you are holding shows its full face whenever it is not over a target.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Poke the keys on an open panel, or speak                                                | Poke with the controller tip, or speak       | Type into the panel that is open: the search panel (opened by the draw pile) or a Note (opened by taking the Note card). Keys and voice do nothing when no panel is open.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

There is no other gesture. Every other action is a card. Pinch, open hand and poke are different hand shapes, so they never stand in for each other.

### Key structures

**Card kinds**

| Kind           | What it is                                                                                                                                                          | Played onto                                                                                                                                                  |
| -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Verb card      | One command with its settings frozen and printed on its face ("PageRank, damping 0.85", "Neighbors, 1 hop", "Keep top 5"); the face lists the object types it takes | Any object of those types; others do not light                                                                                                               |
| Modifier card  | One setting ("damping 0.7", "top 10", "at least 0.07", "weighted by marriages", "or", "off")                                                                        | A played verb card; it replaces that card's setting and the effect is redone, with a ghost first                                                             |
| Selection card | The nodes and edges you pinched, listed on its face; always in the first slot of the fan. Playing it plays a copy; your selection stays in the slot                 | A verb card in the fan or a played one (scopes it to the selection), or the graph's name plate (keeps it as a named group)                                   |
| Result face    | What a played run card turns into: its top values and counts                                                                                                        | It is an object, so other cards play onto it (Color by, Size by, Keep where, Compare)                                                                        |
| Recipe card    | A set of played cards copied into one card by the Copy card                                                                                                         | Any target the first card of the set accepts; it previews the whole set as one ghost, then lays the cards down one by one, so each can still be lifted alone |
| Note card      | Text you type or dictate into it                                                                                                                                    | Any object, including another played card                                                                                                                    |
| Version card   | "Save version": a named point you can return to; the project itself is kept after every play                                                                        | The graph's name plate                                                                                                                                       |

**The deal: what the fan holds, by working object.** Your working object is the last object you pinched; a played run makes its result your working object. The deal changes only when your dominant hand is empty and away from the fan. Cards you drew from the pile stay in the fan until you discard them.

| Working object       | Dealt cards (besides selection, Note, Copy)                                              |
| -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Graph                | Summary, PageRank, Communities, Degree, Find path, Save version                          |
| Node                 | Inspect, Neighbors 1 hop, Neighbors 2 hops, Path to selection, Hide, Pin                 |
| Edge                 | Inspect, Hide, Path through here, Weight by                                              |
| Selection or group   | Neighbors of all, Inspect all, Hide, Path between (when it holds two nodes)              |
| Run result           | Color by, Size by, Keep top 5, Keep above mean, Compare with previous, Label top 5       |
| Style or filter card | Off, and its modifier cards (palette copies, reverse, top 10, top quarter, at least ...) |
| Note                 | Edit (reopens its text panel)                                                            |

Project verbs (Export, Open another project, Rename) live in the draw pile under "Project".

**Where played cards sit.** A card played onto a node or edge sits on a small tab beside it; a hidden node leaves its tab floating where it was, as a faint ring, so the card that hid it can still be lifted. A card played onto the graph sits in the row on the base's front edge, in the order played; that row is the analysis: runs, filter steps and style layers, first to last. Cards played onto a played card hang off it as a short shingled strip, in the order played. The row also shows a small count chip for every node that carries cards; opening a chip (open hand near it) shows that node's cards so you can lift one without reaching into the graph.

**What a lift takes with it.** Lifting a card removes its effect and the effects of the cards played onto it; they travel with it as a small stack in your hand. Cards later in the row that used its output (a filter on its values, a run on a graph it filtered) are redone without it. Everything that would change lights while you pinch, before you pull. Lifting a card and playing it again puts it last, which is how you reorder style layers and filter steps.

### The journey

1. **Enter.**
   **What you do:** Open the Florentine families in graphty on the headset, press Enter VR, and raise your off hand palm up.
   **What you see:** The graph floats at chest height on its base, with "Florentine families" on the name plate. A fan opens above your palm, dealt for the graph: an empty selection card, Note, Copy, Summary, PageRank, Communities, Degree, Find path, Save version.
   **Controls:** Off hand palm up.

2. **Get oriented.**
   **What you do:** Pinch the Summary card out of the fan, hold it over the name plate until its border fills, then release. Push the base rim with an open hand to turn the graph.
   **What you see:** While you hold the card there, a ghost of its face appears on the base: 15 families, 20 marriages, one connected piece. On release the Summary card takes the first place in the row with those facts. The graph turns as you push and stops when you let go.
   **Controls:** Pinch (take), move (aim), release (play), open hand on the rim (turn).

3. **Find the Medici.**
   **What you do:** Pinch the draw pile at the end of the fan. A search panel opens on the ledge; say or poke "Medici". Pinch the Medici card that rises, hold it over the graph, then release it away from the graph.
   **What you see:** The search matches family names as well as commands, so the Medici come up as a node card. Held over the graph, it turns the graph (slowly, about the vertical axis only) until the Medici node faces you. Released over nothing, the card joins your selection card: the Medici are now selected and your working object, and the fan is re-dealt for a node.
   **Controls:** Pinch (open search), voice or poke keys (search), pinch, move (the graph brings the named node to the front), release over nothing (into the selection).

4. **Who they married into.**
   **What you do:** Take "Neighbors, 1 hop" and hold it over the Medici node until the border fills; release.
   **What you see:** Before release, a ghost lights the six families the Medici married into and the six marriages. After release the card sits on a tab beside the Medici, its face listing Acciaiuoli, Albizzi, Barbadori, Ridolfi, Salviati and Tornabuoni, and those families stay highlighted.
   **Controls:** Pinch, move, release.

5. **Who matters most (PageRank).**
   **What you do:** Pinch the name plate to make the graph your working object, take "PageRank, damping 0.85" from the re-dealt fan, hold it over the name plate, release.
   **What you see:** Only the name plate lights for this card; nodes do not, because PageRank runs on a graph. The ghost previews the ranking as node sizes (on a large graph, where the run would be slow, the ghost shows the element's estimate of how long it will take instead). On release the card joins the row and turns into its result face. The fan is re-dealt for the result: Color by, Size by, Keep top 5, Keep above mean, Compare with previous, Label top 5.
   **Controls:** Pinch (graph as working object), pinch, move, release.

6. **Read the result.**
   **What you do:** Hold your open hand near the PageRank card in the row. Then take Inspect and hold it over the Pazzi without letting the border fill; move it back to the fan.
   **What you see:** The card's face opens toward you: Medici about 0.145, Guadagni 0.098, Strozzi 0.088, Albizzi 0.079, Tornabuoni 0.071. Inspect over the Pazzi shows a ghost label "PageRank 0.036, 1 marriage"; released back over the fan, nothing is pinned.
   **Controls:** Open hand near (read), pinch, move (the ghost is the reading), release over nothing.

7. **Color and size by PageRank.**
   **What you do:** Take "Color by", hold it over the PageRank card in the row, release. Do the same with "Size by".
   **What you see:** Color by lights only cards that produced values (the PageRank card; not Summary). While held, the graph shows the coloring as a ghost. On release the colors stay and Color by hangs off the PageRank card. Size by hangs after it, and the families grow by rank. Each is a style layer; its place in the strip is its place in the layer order.
   **Controls:** Pinch, move, release (twice).

8. **Keep only strong families (filter).**
   **What you do:** Take "Keep top 5", hold it over the PageRank card, release. Then take the "top 10" modifier from the re-dealt fan, hold it over the Keep card, look at the ghost, and put it back.
   **What you see:** Held over the PageRank card, Keep top 5 outlines the ten families it would remove. On release they go, and the card reads "Keep top 5: 5 of 15". The top 10 modifier previews ten kept; you decline it. For an exact cut you would open the draw pile and type "0.07": the pile offers an "at least 0.07" modifier, which you would play onto the Keep card.
   **Controls:** Pinch, move, release; release over nothing to decline.

9. **Write a note.**
   **What you do:** Take the Note card. Its text panel opens on the ledge; poke or dictate "Kept 5: the Medici lead by a wide margin". Pinch the finished note and play it onto the Keep card.
   **What you see:** The note hangs off the filter step it explains, so anyone who opens the project finds the reason next to the rule.
   **Controls:** Pinch, poke keys or voice (only while the Note panel is open), pinch, move, release.

10. **Undo a mistake.**
    **What you do:** You meant to Inspect the Strozzi but played Hide; the Strozzi vanished, leaving a faint ring with its tab. Pinch the Hide card on that tab (or the "last play" chip on the fan) and pull it away.
    **What you see:** As you pinch, only the Strozzi's ring lights: that is all the lift would change. As you pull, the Strozzi return, and the Hide card is in your hand to discard. The filter, the colors, the sizes and the note stay as they were: undo removed only that card's effect, even though the Hide came after them. Had you pinched the PageRank card instead, Color by, Size by, Keep and the note would have lit as dependents; releasing before the pull leaves everything in place, and lifting it would bring all of them into your hand as one stack that a single play puts back.
    **Controls:** Pinch on a played card, pull (lift).

11. **Save.**
    **What you do:** Nothing is needed to keep the work: the project is kept after every play. To mark this point, pinch the name plate, take "Save version", and play it onto the name plate.
    **What you see:** A version card joins the row reading "Kept 5, colored by PageRank", the text taken from the cards before it (open it to rename it by poking).
