Reopening the project lays every played card back on its object. Lifting the version card removes that named point, not the work; it comes back to your hand, and playing it again restores it, like any other card.
    **Controls:** Pinch, move, release.

### Advanced journeys

- **Compound rule.** Two filter cards in the row are an AND: play Keep top 5 onto the PageRank card and "Keep at least 3 marriages" onto a Degree result card, and the graph shows families that pass both. For an OR, play the "or" modifier onto the second Keep card: it joins that card to the filter just before it in the row, a bracket links the two, and the second card's face reads "or at least 3 marriages". Any clause is removed by lifting its card, and the ghost shows the families that would come back before you pull.
- **Tuning algorithm options.** Play "damping 0.7" onto the played PageRank card: the ghost shows the new ranking, and on release the run is redone, its face updates, and the cards that hang off it are redone in turn. Values not in the deal come from typing in the draw pile ("damping 0.72" mints that modifier). Options with a fixed set of choices come as one modifier per choice ("weighted by marriages", "personalized from selection").
- **Comparing two runs.** Play a second PageRank card with damping 0.5 onto the name plate, then play "Compare with previous" onto it. Its face lists the families whose rank changed, with arrows, and while it is held the ghost colors the graph by the difference. Lifting the Compare card ends the comparison and leaves both runs.
- **Layers on subsets.** Pinch the Medici and their six partners into the selection card, then play the selection card onto Color by in the fan (or onto a played Color by). The style layer paints only those seven families; the copy of the selection card hangs off the style card, so the scope is visible and liftable. Play "off" onto a style card to hide the layer without losing it; lift "off" to show it again.
- **Recipes.** Play Copy onto the PageRank card: a recipe card "PageRank 0.85, color by, size by, keep top 5" comes into your hand and into the draw pile's recipes. Played onto another project's name plate, it ghosts the whole set first, then lays the cards down one by one, so each part can still be lifted alone.
- **Joining tables.** Stays on the 2D page: choosing a file and matching its columns to node ids is form work. The joined columns then come into VR as new "Color by <column>", "Size by <column>" and "Keep where <column>" cards in the deal for the graph.

### How it scales

- **60+ algorithm catalog.** Only six or so are dealt at once; the rest live in the draw pile, found by typing, by a spoken word, or by a category shown when the pile opens. A drawn card stays in your fan until you discard it, so a session's own working set builds up there. Each algorithm's card plays only onto the object types it takes (a graph, a node, a selection holding a pair of nodes), so the lighting tells you where it can go before you learn it.
- **New object types.** A new object (a layout run, an imported table, a saved view) brings a row in the deal table and a list of target types on each card that accepts it. The vocabulary does not grow: it is taken, aimed and played onto like everything else, and a card played on it sits on it.
- **Plugins.** A plugin algorithm or layout becomes a verb card from its catalog entry, with its default settings frozen on the face, plus one modifier card per option choice and a handful of preset values per number; any other value is minted by typing in the draw pile. Plugins ship cards; they never ship new gestures.
- **Bigger graphs.** Hundreds of nodes make each node a smaller drop target. The armed-target border and ghost show exactly which node a card will land on, and a search or selection card brings a named node to the front; past a few hundred nodes, scoping by selection card replaces aiming at single nodes.
- **The limit.** Continuous tuning: anything you want to drag until it looks right (layout forces, a color ramp's midpoint, a time slider) becomes a run of separate plays, each redoing the effect. A busy node with many cards is read through its count chip in the row, and an analysis of 40 plays makes a long row. Past roughly a dozen cards on one object, a 2D page or a dial (Palette Hand, Tool Belt) does the job better.

### On each device

| Device                                     | Inputs used                                                                | Honest notes on what WebXR exposes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Meta Quest 3 / 3S, hands                   | Hand joints: pinch, open palm, index poke, palm orientation                | Quest Browser exposes all 25 hand joints per hand to WebXR, which is all this needs; pinch, open hand and poke are computed from joint distances, and contact with the rim from the palm joints. Tracking degrades when one hand passes behind the other, so the fan opens upward from the palm and cards are taken from its top edge. No eye tracking on these models. Speech recognition inside an immersive session is not dependable, so search and notes work with the poke keyboard alone. No haptics for hands; each step has a short sound.                                                                                                                                   |
| Meta Quest, Touch controllers              | Trigger (take, play), grip (rim), off stick (scroll), poke with the tip    | Controllers are exposed as gamepads with poses; the fan rides on the off controller. Aim is the controller tip, steadier than a tracked pinch. Quest Browser supports the gamepad vibration pulse, used for armed, played and lifted.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Samsung Galaxy XR, hands                   | Same as Quest hands                                                        | Chrome on Android XR exposes hand joints to WebXR. The device tracks eyes, but the prototype does not rely on gaze reaching the page. Chrome's speech recognition may work for search and notes; it stays optional.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Samsung Galaxy XR, controllers             | Same as Quest controllers                                                  | The optional controllers appear as standard WebXR gamepads; mapping and vibration as on Quest where supported.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Apple Vision Pro, eyes and hands           | Hand joints for everything near; the system's look-and-pinch for far nodes | Safari's WebXR gives hand joints after the person grants permission, and a "transient pointer" that reports a look-aimed ray only at the moment of a pinch. Every pinch may arrive both ways, so the page uses the joint-computed pinch when the hand is within reach of a card, the fan, the rim or the name plate, and the transient pointer only when it is not: then a look and a pinch on a far node toggles it in the selection. Aiming a held card is done with the hand; far targets are brought forward by turning the rim or by a search or selection card. Dictation in an immersive page is not guaranteed; the poke keyboard is the baseline. No haptics; sound instead. |
| Apple Vision Pro, PS VR2 Sense controllers | Trigger, grip, stick as on Quest                                           | visionOS supports these controllers; where Safari exposes them to WebXR as gamepads, the controller mapping applies unchanged. Where it does not, hands are the only path.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

### Why it should work

- **Card games already taught verb-first targeting to millions.** Hearthstone and Slay the Spire both have you pick a card, drag it, see the valid targets light and the effect previewed on the target (Slay the Spire shows the damage number on the enemy before you release), and release to commit. The skill transfers directly.
- **The record on the object has a shipped analytics precedent.** Tableau's shelves hold the whole analysis as visible pills; dragging a pill off its shelf removes just that part of the view. People learn that without instruction. Here the shelf is the object itself.
- **Live preview before commit is proven to cut trial and error.** Office's Live Preview and the hover previews in Figma and Photoshop let people see a style before applying it; the ghost on hover is the same thing on the graph.
- **Selective undo matches how analysts talk about mistakes.** People say "take that filter off", not "undo three steps". Research on selective and regional undo (Berlage 1994; later work on regional undo in editors) found that being able to undo one earlier action while keeping later ones is wanted and understandable when the dependencies are visible, which the lighting on pinch provides. Because a lifted card carries its dependents and a single play restores them, a lift is never a loss.
- **An off-hand palette is a settled XR pattern.** Tilt Brush's off-hand palette and the palm-up hand menus of MRTK and Quest's own system UI show that holding a command surface in the non-dominant hand while the dominant hand works is comfortable and fast for short sessions; the ledge covers the longer ones.
- **Discrete settings are easier to read back than continuous ones.** A card that says "damping 0.7" can be read by anyone later; a dial position cannot. HyperCard and recipe cards showed that a stored action on a card is a unit people can collect, copy and share.

### What it would teach us

- Will analysts accept choosing the verb before the target, once the object they pinched has dealt the verbs? Does the ghost preview make choosing the target last feel natural?
- Does leaving cards on their objects make the state of an analysis readable at a glance, or does it clutter the graph past usefulness? At what count of cards per object does it break?
- Is per-object undo understood when effects depend on each other (lifting PageRank takes its colors with it)? Do people read the lighting before they pull, and do they discover that playing the lifted stack again restores it?
- Can parameter entry live without any continuous control? How often do people want a slider, and for which settings?
- Is a search or selection card turning the graph to its node helpful, or does an object turning on its own disorient people?
- Is a deal by working object predictable, or do people lose a card when the fan re-deals?
- Can card faces be read at arm's length on each headset, and how short must the text be? Do people use the open-hand read, or try to pinch a played card to read it?
- How long do people keep the fan on the hand before moving it to the ledge?

### Known risks

- **Clutter of played cards.** Many cards on one node hide the node. Mitigation: a played card on a node collapses to a small tab showing a count; it opens only when your open hand comes near it, and every node's cards can also be opened and lifted from its count chip in the row.
- **The graph turning on its own causes discomfort.** Mitigation: only a card that names a node turns the graph, only after it has been held still for a moment, only about the vertical axis, at a capped speed; it never moves the graph toward or away from you and can be switched off. The rim always works.
- **Accidental plays when a pinch drops.** Hand tracking sometimes reports a release you did not make. Mitigation: a play needs the card held over a lit target until its border fills; a release before that, or elsewhere, returns the card; and any play is undone by lifting the card or the "last play" chip.
- **Reaching the back of the graph and far tabs.** At 75 cm, the far side of the graph is out of reach. Mitigation: turn the rim, bring a node forward with a search or selection card, lift node cards from the row's count chips, and on Vision Pro take far nodes with look and pinch.
- **Exact numbers are awkward.** Typing a value into the draw pile is slower than a slider for exploration. Mitigation: deal relative modifiers (top 5, top quarter, above mean) that cover most needs; mint exact values by typing; measure how often people need them.
- **Slow previews.** A ghost of a run means running it before you commit, which is slow on a big graph. Mitigation: style and filter cards over existing values preview live; a run card ghosts its result only when it is fast, and otherwise shows the estimated time, and a run started by the ghost is kept so the play is instant.
- **Lifting a lower card surprises people.** Taking off PageRank removes everything built on it. Mitigation: everything that would change lights the moment you pinch, the lift happens only after a 4 cm pull, letting go earlier cancels, and the lifted card brings its dependents into your hand so one play puts them all back.
- **The fan re-deals while you reach.** A card can move from under your fingers. Mitigation: the deal changes only when your dominant hand is empty and away from the fan.
- **Gorilla arm, one-handed and seated use.** Holding the off hand up is tiring, and some people have one usable hand. Mitigation: palm down puts the fan on the ledge in front of you, within reach seated or standing, and one-handed mode keeps it there. Turning needs one hand; the only two-handed act is scaling, which a "Focus" card from the draw pile replaces (played onto a node or selection, it frames it; lifting it restores the view).
- **Text panels need a free hand.** You cannot type while pinching a card. Mitigation: taking the Note card or opening the draw pile puts a panel on the ledge and leaves your hand empty to poke; you pinch the finished note to carry it.

### Comparison row

| #   | Name          | One-line idea                                                                                                                                        | Mode model                                                                     | Navigation                                                                           | Selection                                                             | Command surface                                                                                | Parameters                                                                | Undo                                                                          |
| --- | ------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 13  | Hand of Cards | Fan of verb cards with frozen settings, dealt by the object you pinched; play a card onto a target and it stays there as the record of what was done | pinch an object to deal its verbs, play a card onto any target; it stays on it | open hand turns the base rim; a search or selection card turns the graph to its node | pinching nodes toggles them in a selection card, played like any card | fan on the hand or a ledge, dealt by object type + draw pile with search + discard + last play | modifier cards played on a card, tuned copies, typed values, recipe cards | lift a played card off its object; it carries its dependents back to the hand |

## Prototype 14: Voodoo Dolls

Frame part of the graph between thumb and finger to pull a hand-size copy of it into your hand, then work by touching copies together and by how you hold and move each one, while the real graph stays put.

A doll is not a picture of the graph: it is a live handle on the part of the real graph it stands for. Whatever you do to a doll happens to that part of the real graph, and the real graph is never grabbed, turned or flown through. Moving dolls only proposes; nothing changes in the real graph until a fingertip presses a card.

### How it differs, layer by layer

| Layer                    | Voodoo Dolls                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Differs from                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Mode model               | Relational proxies. A verb that takes two things comes from touching one doll to another (path, compare, combine, color by a result). A verb that takes one thing comes from how a doll is held and moved (shake, stretch, fold, turn over). The number of objects a verb needs decides where it lives. Motions propose; pressing a card with a fingertip does.                                                                                  | Every one of the 11 works on one object first: select then command (Palette Hand, Point and Say, Gesture Grammar), tools (Tool Belt, Workshop, Grip and Tool), object then menu (Context Menus, HUD). None makes a two-object verb out of bringing two objects together.                                                                                 |
| Navigation               | None on the camera or the graph. You pull copies of regions to you, each scaled to about 30 cm across, and turn them in your hand. A doll grows as you bring it toward your eyes, so it never has to come closer than about 35 cm.                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Palette Hand and Gesture Grammar grab the graph with two hands; Tool Belt and Workshop turn it on a turntable; Workbench steers the view with a miniature; HUD flies the view by looking. Here the view never changes.                                                                                                                                   |
| Selection                | Image-plane framing: hold thumb and finger apart so a region sits between them as you see it, then close them. Nearly closed over one node or edge takes that one. Two hands making corners frame a large region. A whole-graph doll is always within reach, so most picks are made inside it, held low, instead of by framing at arm's length. Inside a doll, a fingertip touch points at an element and a pinch-and-pull takes a smaller doll. | Point and pinch (Palette Hand), "this" (Point and Say), lasso (Gesture Grammar, Context Menus), painting (Tool Belt), tongs (Workshop), claw and scoop (Grip and Tool), rings (Spellcasting), lock-on (HUD). No ray, no lasso, no reticle.                                                                                                               |
| Menus / command surfaces | Two-object verbs appear as cards in the gap between two touching dolls. With one doll, shake, stretch and fold propose verbs. Turning a doll over shows all of its verbs as tiles on its base, including a tile for every motion, so the base doubles as the cheat sheet.                                                                                                                                                                        | Palette Hand's palette belongs to the hand, not the object; Context Menus open at the object but list one object's verbs. Nothing in the 11 shows verbs between two objects.                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Parameter entry          | The distance between two dolls that touched and were then pulled apart (a tether) sets a count such as hop depth; how far a doll is stretched sets a threshold. A number option is itself a doll you stretch.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Sliders (Palette Hand), spoken numbers (Point and Say), wrist twist (Gesture Grammar), stick dial (Tool Belt), typing (Workbench), finger counts (Hand Signs), knobs (Grip and Tool). Gesture Grammar's pull distance runs from one node to the hand; here the distance is between two held objects and is the value of the relation between them.       |
| Feedback                 | While you aim a frame, the real graph lights what lies between thumb and finger. While you hold a doll, the real graph lights the region it stands for. A proposal (two touching dolls, a stretch, a fold, a doll raised to throw) previews on the doll and on the real graph; a fingertip resting on a card previews that card; pressing it does it.                                                                                            | HUD previews at the view edges; Context Menus preview inside the menu. Here the preview appears where the two inputs meet.                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Undo                     | Throw a doll over your shoulder to undo what was done through it; each doll keeps its own history. Every doll's base has Undo and Redo tiles; on the whole-graph doll they cover the whole project, so undo is always one reach away.                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Palette button (Palette Hand), "undo" (Point and Say), thumb swipe (Gesture Grammar), B button (Tool Belt), Ctrl+Z (Workbench), receipts (Workshop), thumbs down (Hand Signs), history strip (Grip and Tool), winding time (Spellcasting), counter (HUD). Undo here is per doll and relative to the body, with the whole-graph doll as the global stack. |

**Setup.** Solid colored background in full VR. The real graph floats at chest height about 75 cm away and never moves. A whole-graph doll waits at a fixed home spot below the real graph's near edge on your off-hand side, at lap height when seated, in reach without leaning. Hands are the primary input on every device. Controllers on Quest and Galaxy XR map to the same vocabulary, and so do PS VR2 Sense controllers on Vision Pro if Safari exposes them. Eyes help only on Vision Pro, where looking at a node and pinching picks it. Voice is optional and only fills text fields; the page's own floating keyboard always works.

### The control vocabulary

Each input has one meaning everywhere, on every kind of doll. Opening your hand never does a verb; it only parks. Every change to the real graph is a card you press.

| Input                                                                                                                                                                | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | With controllers                                                          |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Frame and close: thumb and finger held apart in your line of sight to the real graph, palm away from you (or two hands making corners for a big region), then closed | Pull a doll of exactly what lay between them, as seen from your dominant eye. Nearly closed over one node or edge, it makes a doll of that element. A pinch made directly on a node of the real graph counts the same.                                                                                             | Hold trigger and drag a box with the ray; trigger tap on one node or edge |
| Pinch inside a doll and pull out                                                                                                                                     | Take a doll of what you pinched: an element or smaller region inside a doll, a doll's base (a copy of the whole doll), a line in a list (a step, an option, a note)                                                                                                                                                | Trigger inside the doll and pull                                          |
| Fingertip resting on an element in a doll                                                                                                                            | Point at it: its name and values show, and it lights on the real graph. Changes nothing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Controller tip touch, or ray hover                                        |
| Fingertip resting on a card or tile                                                                                                                                  | Preview what it does, on the doll and the real graph                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Tip touch or ray hover                                                    |
| Press a card or tile in with a fingertip                                                                                                                             | Do it. This is the only input that changes the real graph, apart from undo and redo.                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Trigger on the card                                                       |
| Hold with a closed hand (the pinch that took it, or a fist on its base); open the hand                                                                               | Carry the doll; open the hand and it stays parked where you left it. A parked doll is an anchor: anything two hands do on a held doll, one hand can do against a parked one.                                                                                                                                       | Grip                                                                      |
| Bring a doll toward your eyes                                                                                                                                        | A closer look only: the doll enlarges and labels and values appear                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Same                                                                      |
| Shake                                                                                                                                                                | Add one ring of neighbors to the doll, one hop per shake. It changes only what the doll stands for, not the graph; the doll's Undo tile takes a hop back. On dolls that are not regions or elements it does nothing and the doll wobbles.                                                                          | Same, with a haptic tick per hop                                          |
| Stretch: both hands on one doll pulled apart, or one hand pulling a parked doll                                                                                      | Propose a new value for the doll's one number: on a region, keep only the top of its ranking measure (a filter); on an option doll, its value; on a step doll, the step's value. Open one hand and the stretch stays where it is with a card ("Keep 4 of 15"); press it to do it. Return to rest length to cancel. | Both grips on one doll                                                    |
| Fold: squeeze the doll between two hands until it is flat (hands about 10 cm apart, not touching), or press a parked doll flat with one palm                         | Propose collapsing its region into one group; press the "Group 7" card to do it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Both grips, push together                                                 |
| Turn over: roll the doll until its base faces up, with your palm down or sideways                                                                                    | Show all of its verbs as tiles on the base; press a tile                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Same; trigger on a tile                                                   |
| Touch two dolls together                                                                                                                                             | Show the verbs for these two things as cards in the gap, the most likely first and already previewing. Open one hand and the dolls stay docked with the cards showing; press one. Pull them apart without a tether and nothing happens.                                                                            | Same, with a haptic bump at contact                                       |
| Pull two touching dolls apart while holding them (or one against a parked doll)                                                                                      | If a verb takes a count, a tether forms and its length sets the count in detents; open a hand and the tether stays with its card ("All paths up to 3 hops"); press it. If no verb takes a count, or you pull past the last detent, the tether snaps and nothing happens.                                           | Same, with detent ticks                                                   |
| Toss a doll toward the real graph, or push it in                                                                                                                     | Put it away; what was done through it stays. The whole-graph doll goes back to its home spot instead.                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Same                                                                      |
| Raise a doll above your shoulder, then throw it back                                                                                                                 | Raised, it lists every step it will undo, including later steps built on them. Thrown, those steps are undone. A step doll undoes only that step and what depends on it.                                                                                                                                           | Same                                                                      |
| Voice (optional)                                                                                                                                                     | Dictation into the open text field (a note, a search); never a command                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Same                                                                      |

### Key structures

**Kinds of doll.** Every object in graphty can become a doll; the figure shows what it is.

| Doll                   | Pulled from                                                                                               | What it looks like in the hand                                                                                                |
| ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Whole graph            | Always out, at its home spot; also by framing the whole graph or pinching another whole-graph doll's base | The whole graph, scaled to about 30 cm, on a base labeled with its counts and its ranking measure                             |
| Region (any selection) | Framing the real graph, or from another doll                                                              | The nodes and edges of the region on a small base labeled with its count and its ranking measure                              |
| Node or edge           | Framing nearly closed over it, or a pinch on it                                                           | The element with stubs of its edges                                                                                           |
| Group                  | Folding a region doll                                                                                     | One group node, with its count                                                                                                |
| Run (a result)         | Running an algorithm                                                                                      | What the algorithm returns: ranked values as a column of names highest at the top, communities as clusters, a path as a chain |
| Step                   | Pinching a line out of a doll's Steps list                                                                | A short label of the action ("Filter: PageRank 0.075 or more")                                                                |
| Style layer            | Pinching a line out of a doll's Layers list                                                               | The layer's name and a swatch of what it paints                                                                               |
| Option                 | Pinching an option off a run's Options list                                                               | The option's name and number on a bar about 20 cm long                                                                        |
| Recipe                 | Pinching the header of a Steps list                                                                       | The list of steps as a strip                                                                                                  |
| Note                   | Pinching a note off an element                                                                            | The note's text                                                                                                               |

**Verbs in the gap (two dolls).** Algorithms and actions that take two inputs appear here, chosen by the kinds of the two dolls, most likely first.

| Two dolls                      | Verbs in the gap                                                                                          |
| ------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Node + node                    | Shortest path; all paths up to k hops (tether); common neighbors; maximum flow                            |
| Node + region                  | Neighbors in the region within k hops (tether); path into the region                                      |
| Region + region                | New region: either, both, first but not second; edges between them                                        |
| Two proposed stretches (rules) | Keep both; keep either; keep first but not second (one filter step)                                       |
| Run + region                   | Color by; size by; color and size by; rank by; apply layout (layout runs); select (a community or a path) |
| Run + run                      | Compare (who moved, by how much); correlation                                                             |
| Option + run                   | Rerun with this value; run a copy with this value                                                         |
| Layer + layer                  | Put above; put below                                                                                      |
| Layer + region                 | Apply only here                                                                                           |
| Recipe + region                | Replay here                                                                                               |
| Note + anything                | Attach the note here                                                                                      |

Running an algorithm never changes the graph; a run's result reaches the graph only through a gap verb you press.

**Tiles on the base (one doll).** Every verb of the doll, including a tile for each motion (Grow for shake, Group for fold, Discard for the throw) for anyone who cannot make the motion. Each motion tile shows its gesture.

| Doll         | Tiles                                                                                                           |
| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Region       | Run (the catalog of one-input algorithms); Rank by; Find; Grow; Group; Note; Steps; Layers; Undo; Redo; Discard |
| Whole graph  | The region tiles, plus Save and Load. Its Undo, Redo and Steps cover the whole project.                         |
| Node or edge | Run from here; Grow; Note; Pin; Steps; Undo; Redo; Discard                                                      |
| Group        | Ungroup; Note; Steps; Undo; Redo; Discard                                                                       |
| Run          | Options; Rerun; Note; Steps; Discard                                                                            |
| Step         | Edit (stretch again or retype its value); Note; Discard                                                         |
| Style layer  | Hide or show; Note; Discard                                                                                     |

Discard does the same as the throw, with the same preview while your fingertip rests on it. Undone steps stay in the Steps list, crossed out, until something new replaces them; Redo or pressing one brings it back. Load saves the current project first.

### The journey

1. **Enter**
    - **What you do:** Put on the headset, open graphty with the Florentine families loaded, press Enter VR, and stand or sit facing the graph. Frame a single dot once so the page learns your dominant eye.
    - **What you see:** The 15 families as nodes with names, at chest height about 75 cm away, on a solid background. Your hands appear, and so does the whole-graph doll at its home spot by your off hand. As your hand rises between your eye and the graph with thumb and finger apart, an outline on the real graph shows what lies between them.
    - **Controls:** One calibration frame; the outline teaches framing before you do it.

2. **Get oriented**
    - **What you do:** Pick up the whole-graph doll in your off hand. Turn it to see the graph from the side and from behind; bring it toward your eyes to read the crowded middle.
    - **What you see:** The real graph does not move. While you hold the doll, the real graph is outlined to show that the doll stands for all of it. The base reads "15 families, 20 marriages".
    - **Controls:** Hold; turn; bring close.

3. **Find the Medici**
    - **What you do:** Hold thumb and finger nearly closed over the Medici on the real graph and close them, or pinch the Medici inside the whole-graph doll and pull. On a large graph, turn the whole-graph doll over, press Find and say or type "Medici"; the found node's doll drops into your free hand.
    - **What you see:** A Medici doll in your dominant hand: the node with six edge stubs. The Medici node lights on the real graph.
    - **Controls:** Frame and close, or pinch and pull out; or turn over and press Find.

4. **Who they married into**
    - **What you do:** Shake the Medici doll once.
    - **What you see:** The doll grows a ring as Acciaiuoli, Albizzi, Barbadori, Ridolfi, Salviati and Tornabuoni join it. The real graph lights those six and the Medici. The base reads "Medici and 6 neighbors, 1 hop". A second shake would add the second ring (the Medici and 11 other families within 2 hops); the doll's Undo tile takes a ring back.
    - **Controls:** Shake.

5. **Who matters most (PageRank)**
    - **What you do:** Toss the Medici doll toward the real graph to put it away. Turn the whole-graph doll over, press Run, and press PageRank. PageRank sits near the top of the list as a common choice; the list scrolls with a fingertip and has search.
    - **What you see:** The run finishes and a PageRank run doll appears in your free hand: the 15 families as a ranked column. The run is also listed under Steps on the whole-graph doll's base. Nothing on the real graph has changed yet.
    - **Controls:** Toss toward the graph; turn over; press tiles.

6. **Read the result**
    - **What you do:** Bring the run doll toward your eyes. Rest a fingertip on the top entries.
    - **What you see:** Medici first (about 0.145), then Guadagni (about 0.098) and Strozzi (about 0.088). Each entry you touch lights on the real graph, so you see where the top families sit.
    - **Controls:** Bring close; fingertip touch.

7. **Color and size by PageRank**
    - **What you do:** Touch the run doll to the whole-graph doll. Cards for "Color by", "Size by", "Color and size by" and "Rank by" appear in the gap. Open the hand holding the run doll so the two stay docked, rest a fingertip on "Color and size by", then press it.
    - **What you see:** While your fingertip rests on the card, the whole-graph doll and the real graph preview the new colors and sizes. After you press, they stay, and the Medici are largest. The whole-graph doll's base now reads "ranked by PageRank": a doll with no ranking measure takes the first measure it is colored or sized by, and Rank by changes it. A new style layer is listed under Layers.
    - **Controls:** Touch two dolls; open a hand; rest a fingertip; press a card.

8. **Keep only strong families (filter)**
    - **What you do:** Take the whole-graph doll in both hands and pull them apart. At the count you want, open one hand and press the card that appears.
    - **What you see:** The doll lines its families up by PageRank along the stretch, and the lowest drop out one at a time. Each step snaps to the gap between two families' values, so the cut never sits on a meaningless number. The real graph fades the dropped families as a preview. At "PageRank 0.075 or more: 4 of 15" (Medici, Guadagni, Strozzi, Albizzi) you open one hand; the stretch holds and a "Keep 4 of 15" card shows on the base. Pressing it makes the filter step.
    - **Controls:** Stretch; open one hand; press the card (returning to rest length cancels).

9. **Write a note**
    - **What you do:** Pinch the Medici out of the whole-graph doll. Turn the Medici doll over, press Note, and dictate "Bridge between the oligarch and new families", or type it on the floating keyboard. Toss the doll toward the real graph.
    - **What you see:** The note appears beside the Medici on the real graph and in every doll that contains the Medici.
    - **Controls:** Pinch and pull out; turn over; press Note; voice or keyboard; toss toward the graph.

10. **Undo a mistake**
    - **What you do:** Suppose your stretch in step 8 went too far and kept only Medici and Guadagni. Turn the whole-graph doll over and press Steps. Pinch the filter step out of the list, raise it above your shoulder, read what it will undo, and throw it back. Then stretch again to 4 of 15 and press Keep. (Seated or with limited shoulder movement, rest a fingertip on the step doll's Discard tile to see the same list, then press it; or press Undo on the whole-graph doll.)
    - **What you see:** Above your shoulder the step doll reads "Undo: Filter PageRank 0.12 or more. Nothing depends on it." After the throw the filter is gone and all 15 families are back; the color and size stay, because they were a separate step. The thrown step stays in the Steps list, crossed out, so Redo or pressing it would bring it back. The new stretch replaces it.
    - **Controls:** Turn over; press Steps; pinch and pull out; raise and throw, or Discard; stretch; press.

11. **Save**
    - **What you do:** Turn the whole-graph doll over and press Save.
    - **What you see:** A short confirmation on the base: "Saved: Florentine families, 3 steps, 1 note". Toss the doll toward the real graph and it returns to its home spot, clearing your hands.
    - **Controls:** Turn over; press Save; toss toward the graph.

### Advanced journeys

- **Compound rule.** A stretched doll you have not pressed Keep on is a rule. Pinch the whole-graph doll's base to pull a copy, so you have two. Stretch one, ranked by PageRank, to keep the top 5, and open a hand without pressing Keep; park it. On the second, press Rank by, choose degree, and stretch to keep families with 3 or more marriages. Touch the two rule dolls together and the gap offers "Keep both", "Keep either" and "Keep first but not second". Rest a fingertip on Keep both to preview it on the real graph, then press it: one filter step keeps only the families that pass both rules.
- **Tuning algorithm options.** Turn the PageRank run doll over and press Options. Pinch "damping 0.85" off the list into an option doll and stretch it to 0.70; it moves in detents of 0.05, or steps of 0.01 when you bring it toward your eyes. Touch the option doll to the run doll and the gap offers "Rerun with 0.70" and "Run a copy with 0.70". Resting on Rerun previews what the colors and sizes built on this run would become.
- **Comparing two runs.** Run a copy with the new damping, then touch the two run dolls together. The first card in the gap is Compare. Its preview shows which families moved up or down and by how much. Press it to keep the comparison as its own run doll, ranked by how far each family moved.
- **Layers on subsets.** Frame the Medici's neighborhood, or shake a Medici doll once, and touch the PageRank run doll to that region doll. "Color by" now makes a style layer that applies only to those seven families. Folding the region doll and pressing Group collapses them into one group node, and the group carries the layer. To change the order of layers, pinch two lines out of the Layers list and touch them together: "Put above" or "Put below".
- **Recipes.** Turn a doll over, press Steps, and pinch the list's header: the whole sequence comes out as a recipe doll. Touch it to a whole-graph doll of another dataset and the gap offers "Replay here", previewing each step before you press. A recipe doll saved with the project can be pulled again later from Load.
- **Joining tables.** This stays on the 2D page, because choosing a file and the matching columns needs reading and typing that dolls do not help with. Once a table is joined, its columns appear under Rank by and in Run, and they can drive stretches and colors in VR.

### How it scales

- **60+ algorithm catalog.** An algorithm's inputs decide where it appears, so no menu is built by hand. One-input algorithms are listed under Run on the base of the matching kind of doll: whole-graph and region dolls get centrality, community and layout, and node dolls get "from here" algorithms such as single-source shortest paths. Two-input algorithms appear in the gap of the matching pair, such as node + node for paths, flow and similarity. The Run list shows recent and pinned algorithms first, then search by voice or keyboard. Options are pinched off as option dolls. What an algorithm returns (scores, groups, a path, positions) decides which gap verbs its run doll offers.
- **New object types.** A new kind of object, such as a new kind of filter step, a table or a comparison, needs a doll figure, its base tiles, and the list of pairs it joins. Shake, stretch, fold, turn over, put away and throw keep their meanings on it, and a motion that has no meaning for it does nothing visibly.
- **Plugins.** A plugin algorithm declares its inputs and outputs, as every catalog entry does, and lands on the right base or in the right gap automatically. A plugin's two-object action, for example "align these two graphs", appears in the gap of its pair.
- **The limit.** A 30 cm doll of thousands of nodes is unreadable, so on a large graph you pull dolls from dolls, two or three levels deep. A verb with three or more inputs needs two dolls combined first. More than about six parked dolls becomes clutter. Many steps take two presses (open a hand, then press a card) where releasing could have been one. A region base carries a dozen tiles. Typing anything longer than a search term is slow without voice.

### On each device

| Device            | Inputs exposed to WebXR                                                                                                                                                                                   | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Meta Quest 3 / 3S | Hands (25 joints per hand, in Meta Quest Browser); Touch Plus controllers with haptics; no eye tracking                                                                                                   | Hands work as written. Tracking drops when one hand covers the other and when a hand goes behind or beside the head. Because opening a hand only parks, a lost frame never does a verb; a lost hand freezes its doll in place. The throw counts as soon as the raised doll moves back past the shoulder line, before the hand leaves tracking. A pinch with the palm toward your face opens the system menu and the page cannot block it, so framing is done palm away and turning over palm down. The page cannot open the system keyboard inside an immersive session, so it draws its own. Controllers give haptic ticks for detents, contact and hops, and make throws and shakes reliable.    |
| Samsung Galaxy XR | Hands and optional controllers through Chrome on Android XR                                                                                                                                               | Same mapping as Quest, including the system palm-up pinch to avoid and the page's own keyboard. The headset tracks eyes for its own interface, but we have not confirmed that Chrome exposes gaze to WebXR pages; this prototype does not need it. Check how well hand joints support image-plane framing and how speech recognition behaves inside an immersive session.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Apple Vision Pro  | Hand tracking (Safari WebXR, on request). Looking at something and pinching arrives as a transient pointer, whose ray points where you looked at the moment of the pinch. No continuous gaze, no haptics. | Framing works with hands. To pick one node, looking at it and pinching with a relaxed hand is easier, and replaces the nearly closed frame. Every pinch also arrives as a transient pointer, so the page ignores its select event whenever the pinch is a frame, is inside a doll, or holds a doll, or one pinch does two things. Looking at an upturned palm brings up the system Home control, so turning over is done palm down. visionOS apps support PS VR2 Sense controllers, but it is unconfirmed whether Safari WebXR exposes them as tracked controllers; if it does, they take the Quest controller column. Without haptics, detents and contact show a visible snap and a short sound. |

### Why it should work

- In Pierce, Stearns and Pausch's Voodoo Dolls (1999), people worked on distant objects by holding hand-size copies in two hands, with one copy as the reference frame for the other. They could act on objects of very different sizes without moving the view.
- Image-plane selection (Pierce et al., 1997) picks distant objects by framing them as you see them, with no ray to aim. Framing a region between thumb and finger is that work's "head crusher" technique.
- Worlds in Miniature (Stoakley, Conway and Pausch, 1995) showed that people readily understand that acting on a hand-held copy changes the full-size world.
- Mine, Brooks and Sequin (1997) proposed actions relative to the body, including deleting an object by throwing it over your shoulder, because proprioception finds the space around your own body without looking. Half-Life: Alyx (2020) taught a large audience to reach over the shoulder for an inventory.
- Many graph questions are relational: a path between two families, a comparison of two runs, the overlap of two groups. Bringing the two things together is the question itself, and the gap shows only verbs that fit that pair.
- Direct touch on a doll held at 35 to 45 cm is steadier than a ray aimed at small targets 75 cm away, where hand-tracking jitter is magnified. Enlarging the doll as it nears the eyes adds precision without a finer gesture and without focusing on something too close.
- Asymmetric two-handed work (Guiard, 1987) fits the doll: one hand holds the frame of reference while the other touches and pinches inside it. A parked doll can take the holding hand's place, so the same verbs work one-handed.
- Motions only propose and a fingertip press does, so trying a stretch, a pairing or a fold is safe, a dropped hand or a tracking loss never commits anything, and the common VR habit of pulling an object apart to enlarge it costs nothing.

### What it would teach us

- Do people understand a doll as a live handle on the real graph, rather than a copy they are editing on the side?
- Does placing verbs by how many objects they take (one on the base, two in the gap) make algorithms easier to find than a catalog organized by family?
- How accurate is image-plane framing with consumer hand tracking at 75 cm, compared with a ray or a lasso, and how much does eye dominance throw it off? How often do people frame at all once the whole-graph doll is always at hand?
- Is per-doll history clearer than one global undo stack, or do people lose track of which doll did what?
- Is stretching a ranked doll an accurate way to set a threshold, and do the snaps between values help or annoy?
- Does pressing a card after a motion feel slow next to committing on release, and do people miss the one-motion version?
- Do people miss moving the view, or does pulling dolls cover every reason to navigate?
- How often do shakes, stretches and throws fire by accident during ordinary gesturing?
- How many dolls do people keep parked, and where do they put them?

### Known risks

- **Framing parallax.** What lies between thumb and finger depends on which eye you use. Mitigation: show the framed region on the real graph before closing, learn eye dominance from one frame at entry, pick inside the whole-graph doll instead, and on Vision Pro pick single nodes by looking and pinching.
- **System gestures.** A pinch with the palm toward your face opens the system menu on Quest and Galaxy XR, and an upturned palm brings up Home on Vision Pro; pages cannot block either. Mitigation: framing is palm away, turning over is palm down, and holding a doll in a fist is always allowed.
- **Accidental shakes.** Ordinary hand waving could add neighbors. Mitigation: require two quick reversals above a speed threshold, let users with a tremor turn shaking off and use Grow, and make the doll's Undo tile take a hop back. A shake never changes the graph, only what the doll stands for.
- **Throwing undoes more than intended, and is hard to track and to make.** A hand behind the head leaves tracking, and seated people or those with limited shoulder movement cannot throw. Mitigation: the raised doll lists every step it will undo, including dependents, before the throw; the throw counts while the hand is still tracked; undone steps stay crossed out and Redo brings them back; Discard and Undo tiles do the same with a fingertip.
- **Hands hide each other** when two dolls touch or a doll is folded. Mitigation: dolls touch at their edges, so the hands holding them stay about 30 cm apart; folding stops short of the hands touching; opening a hand never commits, so a lost frame is harmless; controllers for long sessions.
- **Clutter.** Many parked dolls crowd the space. Mitigation: when more than four are parked they shrink and line up to one side, tossing a doll toward the graph puts it away, and the whole-graph doll shows how many dolls are out.
- **Which measure a stretch uses.** Mitigation: the base always names its ranking measure, and the stretch shows the cut value and the count kept.
- **Dense regions are unreadable at 30 cm.** Mitigation: pull dolls from dolls; labels appear only as the doll nears the eyes.
- **Eye strain from close objects.** Text held closer than about 35 cm is tiring to focus on in a headset. Mitigation: the doll enlarges as it approaches, so labels are readable at arm's half-length.
- **Arm fatigue** from holding dolls up and framing at arm's length. Mitigation: parked dolls stay where they are left, so the arms can drop; the whole-graph doll sits at lap height; everything except framing and the throw works with elbows at the sides.
- **Shaking means undo on phones.** People may expect a shake to undo. Mitigation: the first shake shows its effect clearly, and undo is never a shake.

### Comparison row

| #   | Name         | One-line idea                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Mode model                                                     | Navigation                          | Selection                                                                                | Command surface                                                                       | Parameters                                                                                 | Undo                                                                                                         |
| --- | ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| 14  | Voodoo Dolls | Frame part of the graph between thumb and finger to pull a hand-size live copy; touch copies together for two-object verbs, hold and move one for one-object verbs; motions propose, a fingertip press does; the real graph stays put | relational proxies: verbs placed by how many objects they take | none; pull copies of regions to you | image-plane framing; pick inside an always-present whole-graph doll; touch inside a doll | cards in the gap between touching dolls; shake, stretch, fold; tiles on a doll's base | tether length between two dolls (hops); stretch length (threshold); stretched option dolls | throw a doll over the shoulder (per doll, with preview); Undo and Redo tiles, global on the whole-graph doll |

## Prototype 15: Physics Lab

The graph is a running simulation: you set force objects into it (attribute magnets, algorithm wells, lamps, bins, fences), watch the nodes move under them, and press an hourglass to freeze time and commit what you see.

### How it differs, layer by layer

| Layer                    | Physics Lab                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Differs from                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Mode model               | Applied laws in a live simulation. Every command is a field object placed in space; it acts for as long as it is there. Time runs until you freeze it, and the freeze is the explicit commit. The next change you make starts time again from the frozen state. Nothing is a one-shot action.                                                                  | Every designed prototype issues discrete commands (select then command in Palette Hand, Point and Say, Gesture Grammar, Context Menus; tools in Tool Belt, Workshop, Grip and Tool; cast in Spellcasting). Workshop has physical tools, but each tool acts once when used; here the law keeps acting until removed. |
| Navigation               | The graph has inertia. Push it with an open palm and it drifts and spins; grab it to catch it, carry it closer, or stop it. A soft tether brings it back only if it drifts out of reach.                                                                                                                                                                       | Two-hand grab (Palette Hand, Gesture Grammar), turntable (Tool Belt, Workshop), miniature (Workbench), radar (HUD), stick (Context Menus). None has momentum.                                                                                                                                                       |
| Selection                | Throw or carry nodes into bins to group them; whatever falls into a well past its rim is kept (a well is a filter); throw a bead out of a bin to remove that node; turn a bin over to pour it out.                                                                                                                                                             | Pointing and lasso (Palette Hand, Gesture Grammar, Context Menus), painting (Tool Belt), tongs (Workshop), lock-on (HUD), ring and sweep (Spellcasting). None selects by throwing or by falling.                                                                                                                    |
| Menus / command surfaces | A rack of field objects in front of you, low on the dominant side: one magnet and one lamp per attribute, one well per algorithm family, blank bins, fences, note slips, the hourglass and the specimen jar; a drawer at the rack's end holds the full catalog. Poking any object opens its readout, which lists that object's facts and its verbs as buttons. | Closest to Tool Belt's cartridge rack and Workshop's pegboard, but every object on this rack is a force or a container, not a tool you operate.                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Parameters               | Twist a held field object to turn its dial (strength); its distance from the graph sets its reach; a well's rim height is its threshold; option pegs on a well twist like a dial. Every value snaps in detents, and poking its readout lets you type or say the exact number.                                                                                  | Sliders (Palette Hand), spoken numbers (Point and Say), wrist twist on an abstract dial (Gesture Grammar), stick dial and trigger pressure (Tool Belt). Here the value is a property of a physical object and you see its effect as motion while you set it.                                                        |
| Feedback                 | Nodes move under the forces with short trails; field lines show each object's reach; a detent tick (rumble on controllers, a click on hands) each time a value steps; a freeze flashes once; readouts by poking.                                                                                                                                               | Every designed prototype shows results as color, labels or lists. Here the primary feedback is motion: you see a result arrive as nodes travel.                                                                                                                                                                     |

**Setup:** fixed environment (solid colored background, full VR, graph floating at chest height about 75 cm away). Meta Quest 3/3S and Samsung Galaxy XR with hands or controllers; Apple Vision Pro with hands (look and pinch for anything out of reach) or PS VR2 Sense controllers. Works standing or seated. Voice is optional and only ever fills an open text field (a name, a note, a search, an exact number); it never issues a command.

### The control vocabulary

| Input            | Hands                                                                                                              | Controllers                                                         | Meaning everywhere                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Grab             | Pinch on an object. Out of reach: aim the hand's pointing ray (Vision Pro: look at it) and pinch and hold          | Grip; out of reach, aim the ray and grip                            | Hold and carry it: a field object, a node, a bin, a note slip, the graph itself. What you will grab highlights before the pinch closes (not on Vision Pro, where it outlines the moment you pinch). A pinch on a node takes the node; a pinch in empty space inside the graph takes the graph. Grabbing a moving graph catches it. A far grab pulls the object to your hand, as gravity gloves do. |
| Release          | Open the pinch                                                                                                     | Release grip                                                        | Released slowly (below a throw speed of about 1 m/s): placed where it is. Released fast: thrown, keeping its velocity. A node is never left where you drop it: it goes back to wherever the laws hold it.                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Drop onto        | Release one object while it touches another (the target lights up first)                                           | Same                                                                | Act on the other object's contents: a lamp on a bin styles only the bin's members, a magnet into a well's drain makes the well sort by that attribute, a bin into a well's funnel counts its members as kept, a note slip on a node or edge annotates it, a recipe bin on the graph places its objects.                                                                                            |
| Lift out         | Carry a field object outside the reach shell (it shows while you hold one) or back to the rack, and release        | Same                                                                | Remove that law. The nodes relax back.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Twist            | Roll the wrist while holding an object that has a dial: a magnet, a well, a lamp, an option peg                    | Same                                                                | Turn its dial: strength, or the option's value. The object stays upright and only the dial turns; it clicks in detents. Past the end of your wrist's comfortable roll, release slowly and regrab to keep turning, like a stiff knob. Anything without a dial simply turns with your hand.                                                                                                          |
| Flip             | Grab the object with both hands and turn it over (the hourglass alone sits in a pivot frame, so one hand turns it) | Both grips; Y (or B on the off hand) flips the hourglass            | Reverse it: a magnet repels, a well becomes a hill (rejects instead of keeps), a bin pours out its members (the group dissolves), the hourglass undoes. During the turn the reversed result previews; it applies only once the object passes halfway over. On the graph, turning it over just turns it.                                                                                            |
| Stretch          | Grab one object with both hands and pull apart or together                                                         | Same, with both grips                                               | Change its size: the graph (zoom), a bin's mouth, a fence's length.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Push             | Open flat palm moving quickly through the graph, while nothing is held                                             | Flick the thumbstick                                                | Give the graph momentum: a sideways flick drifts it, an off-center push spins it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
