On Vision Pro, you aim at whatever you are looking at when you pinch. Every input works seated and, except the two-hand pull, with one hand; the two-hand pull has a one-handed equivalent. Voice is optional: it only mirrors the three strip buttons and fills in text.

### The control vocabulary

Every input has one meaning. The system decides what that meaning implies here and shows it as a guess. Anything you act on (tap, drag, stretch, color, flick) joins the example tray. Accepting a guess clears the tray.

| Input                                                                                                                                   | Meaning everywhere                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Point (the system target ray of each hand or controller, usable with hands resting low; on Vision Pro, gaze at the moment of the pinch) | Aim. The aimed thing gets a thin outline and its name. The ray snaps to the nearest node within a few degrees so small nodes are easy to hit. Nothing changes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Pinch tap a thing (release after moving less than about 2 cm)                                                                           | "Like this": it becomes an example in the tray. Pinch tap an example again to drop it. A thing can be a node, an edge, the graph itself (tap empty space inside the graph's outline), a rule row, a note or the project chip.                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Flick a thing (pinch, throw it away from you or down, let go fast), or drag it onto the set-aside ledge                                 | "Not this": it should be out of what you end up with. With a selection guess pending, it is a counter-example, crossed out in the tray. With nothing pending, or with a hide guess pending, the guess is to hide it and things like it, and the preview shows them on the ledge. Nothing is hidden until you Accept.                                                                                 |
| Pinch-drag a thing and let go slowly                                                                                                    | "Put it here". Where it lands tells the system why: a tray thumbnail dropped above another (it ranks above), a node dropped outside the graph toward you (bring it to the front), a node dropped inside the graph (move just this node), the graph dropped anywhere (move the graph), a ghost dragged up off the ledge (it should not be hidden), a material or tape dropped on a thing (see below). |
| Two-hand pinch, pull apart or push together                                                                                             | "This much". It acts on what the first hand pinched: a node gets bigger or smaller, the graph zooms. If the first hand pinched empty air, it acts on the tray's examples together: frame these, spread these. A second pinch during a drag turns the drag into a pull. One-handed: drag the corner handle of a tray thumbnail.                                                                       |
| Drag a material from the shelf onto a thing                                                                                             | "This color" (a swatch) or "a note here" (the blank note).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Tap a strip button                                                                                                                      | Accept, Next guess, or No. With nothing pending, No reads "Take back: <the last change>" and previews undoing it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Tap a chip                                                                                                                              | Open its alternatives (other measures, other values, other scopes). The verb chip lists every verb of the subject object, so any verb is reachable without a demonstration.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Pinch a value chip and slide                                                                                                            | An exact value.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Tap Record                                                                                                                              | Start or stop recording a tape. The strip shows "recording" while it runs.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Voice (optional): "accept", "next", "reject", dictation                                                                                 | The three buttons; text into a note or a chip.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

Controller mapping (Quest, Galaxy XR):

- trigger is pinch
- the controller ray is point
- flick is a throw with the trigger held, released fast
- both triggers are the two-hand pull
- on the dominant controller, the lower face button (A or X) is Accept, the upper (B or Y) is No / Take back, stick right is Next guess
- grips and the off-hand buttons are not used, so they cannot misfire

### Key structures

**The rule strip**

| Part                        | What it holds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| --------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Project chip (left end)     | The project's name and "saved". Pinch tap it to make the project the example; its verb chip then lists rename, save a copy, export and settings.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Example tray                | Thumbnails of the current examples, up to 8; counter-examples crossed out. Pinch tap one to drop it; drag one above another to show an order; drag a corner handle to show "this much" with one hand. A Clear mark at the end empties it.                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| The guess                   | The inferred rule in plain words, as chips: verb, subject, condition, value, scope. Example: [hide] [families] [PageRank] [below 0.04] [in: whole graph]. The next two guesses are named in small type beside it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Reason line                 | Why this guess: "your examples were the 4 lowest by PageRank; also fits: fewer than 2 marriages".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Count and borderline        | "changes 4 of 15; borderline: Barbadori (0.050), Salviati (0.061)".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Accept / Next guess / No    | The only buttons. Next guess cycles the ranked alternatives; No drops the guess and keeps the examples, and the system offers nothing more from them until you change the examples. With nothing pending, No becomes Take back.                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Materials shelf             | 8 labeled color swatches, a blank note and your recorded tapes. Materials are not commands; they are what you demonstrate with.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Record                      | Toggles recording of a tape.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Rule list (under the strip) | Accepted rules, newest first, each a row of chips. Every accepted rule is a style layer, filter step, selection, run or note. A row is an object like any other: pinch tap it and it joins the tray, becomes current (its matches outlined, its chips editable), and its first guess is "edit this rule". Flick a row away and the guess is "retract this rule", previewed. View changes are not listed, but Take back covers them. |

**What you do and what the system guesses**

| Demonstration                                              | Guesses offered (first guess first)                                 |
| ---------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| One node tapped                                            | Its neighbors; nodes like this; frame this                          |
| The graph tapped                                           | Frame everything; reset the view                                    |
| Two nodes tapped                                           | Nodes like these (shared facts); the path between them; frame these |
| Two examples, then both hands pinch the air and pull apart | Frame these; spread this region                                     |
| Counter-example flicked while a selection guess is pending | The same rule, narrowed                                             |
| A thing flicked with nothing pending, then more flicked    | Hide things like these                                              |
| One tray thumbnail dragged above another                   | Rank everything by a measure that agrees with that order            |
| One example stretched bigger, another pushed smaller       | Size by a measure that agrees                                       |
| Swatches dropped on one or two examples                    | Color by a measure or a group that agrees                           |
| A node dragged out of the graph toward you                 | Bring it to the front; move just this node                          |
| A node dragged within the graph                            | Move just this node; bring it to the front                          |
| Blank note dropped on a thing                              | A note on this one; a note on these                                 |
| A rule row tapped                                          | Edit, extend or narrow that rule                                    |
| A rule row flicked                                         | Retract that rule                                                   |
| Two run rows tapped                                        | Compare ranks; difference; where they disagree                      |

**Views and data changes.** A guess that only changes the view (bring to front, frame, move or zoom the graph) applies live as you move, so you see it while you do it; letting go keeps it, and No puts the view back. Every other guess (selection, run, style layer, filter, note, node move) is only a preview until you Accept, and a preview you leave alone is dropped, never applied, when you start another demonstration.

**The set-aside ledge.** A strip under the graph that holds whatever filters have hidden, as small ghosts, with the borderline ones first. While a hide guess is pending, the things it would hide appear there as outlines. Drag a ghost back up to say "this should not have been hidden".

**The recorder.** Tap Record, work, tap Record again. A tape is the demonstrations together with the guesses you accepted. It has input slots for the examples it started from, so you replay it by dragging it from the shelf onto new examples. Each recorded guess replays as a pending guess with its preview. Tapes are for reuse only; undo never plays a tape backward.

### The journey

1. **Enter.**
   **What you do:** Put on the headset and enter VR from the graphty page.
   **What you see:** The 15 Florentine families and their 20 marriages float in front of you. The rule strip says "Show me: pinch one or two families". The tray is empty.
   **Controls:** None.

2. **Get oriented.**
   **What you do:** Look around the graph. To see the families at the back, pinch-drag Lamberteschi out of the graph toward you and let go slowly.
   **What you see:** Because it landed outside the graph, toward you, the guess is "bring Lamberteschi to the front". It is a view change, so the graph turns and slides as you drag, over about half a second, until Lamberteschi faces you; Next guess would be "move just this node", and No would put the view back. To see the whole graph again, pinch tap empty space inside the graph's outline: the graph becomes the example and its first guess, "frame everything", applies. To frame part of it, pinch tap two families and then pinch the air with both hands and pull apart: "frame these".
   **Controls:** Pinch-drag ("put it here"), pinch tap, two-hand pull ("this much"), Next guess, No.

3. **Find the Medici.**
   **What you do:** Point across the graph; each family you aim at is outlined and shows its name (on Vision Pro the name appears when you pinch). Pinch tap the Medici.
   **What you see:** The Medici join the tray. The strip shows their facts (6 marriages), the first guess, "families married to the Medici", and beside it the next two: "families like the Medici", "frame the Medici". The six in-laws are outlined as a preview. Nothing is applied yet.
   **Controls:** Point, pinch tap.

4. **Who they married into.**
   **What you do:** With the Medici in the tray, pinch tap one in-law, Ridolfi.
   **What you see:** The guess stays "families married to the Medici", now with the reason "Ridolfi married a Medici; so did 5 others". Six families are outlined: Acciaiuoli, Albizzi, Barbadori, Ridolfi, Salviati, Tornabuoni. You Accept, the selection becomes a row in the rule list, and the tray clears.
   **Controls:** Pinch tap, Accept.

5. **Who matters most (PageRank).**
   **What you do:** Pinch tap the Medici and Pazzi. In the tray, drag the Medici's thumbnail above Pazzi's: this one matters more than that one.
   **What you see:** The first guess is [rank] [families] [by degree], with the reason "the Medici have 6 marriages, Pazzi 1". As a preview, each family rises to a height set by its degree. You tap the [by degree] chip. Its alternatives are the measures that agree with your order, each with a small preview: betweenness, PageRank, closeness, eigenvector, and "more measures" (searchable). You pick PageRank, the preview re-ranks, and you Accept. The run is added to the rule list and stays current.
   **Controls:** Pinch tap, drag in the tray, tap a chip, Accept.

6. **Read the result.**
   **What you do:** Point at families one after another.
   **What you see:** While the run is current, each family stays lifted to its PageRank height, and the top ten carry value labels. The family you aim at shows its value and place: the Medici are about 0.14, first of 15; Guadagni second; Strozzi third; Pazzi near the bottom. The count line reads "top 3: Medici, Guadagni, Strozzi". On Vision Pro, which has no hover, pinch tap a family to see its value and place in the tray, and tap it again to drop it. To make the run current again later, pinch tap its row in the rule list.
   **Controls:** Point (pinch tap on Vision Pro).

7. **Color and size by PageRank.**
   **What you do:** Pinch the Medici with one hand, pinch the air with the other and pull apart; then pinch Pazzi and push your hands together. (With one hand: drag the corner handles of their tray thumbnails.) Drag the red swatch onto the Medici and the blue onto Pazzi.
   **What you see:** After the stretch, the guess is "size by PageRank", with the reason "you made the first ranked bigger and the last smaller; the PageRank run is current". Every family previews at its new size. After the swatches, the chips grow to [size and color] [blue to red] [by PageRank]. You Accept, and it becomes a style layer.
   **Controls:** Two-hand pull (or tray handle), drag a material, Accept.

8. **Keep only strong families (filter).**
   **What you do:** Flick Pazzi away, then Ginori. (Or drag each onto the ledge.)
   **What you see:**
    - The first flick has nothing pending, so the guess is "hide families like Pazzi", and Pazzi appears as an outline on the ledge.
    - The second flick refines it to [hide] [families] [PageRank] [below 0.04]. The reason line says "your 2 examples are among the 4 lowest; also fits: fewer than 2 marriages, which hides the same 4 today".
    - The 4 previewed on the ledge are Acciaiuoli, Ginori, Lamberteschi and Pazzi. Barbadori, at 0.050, is named as borderline.
    - You Accept, and the 4 move to the set-aside ledge.
      **Controls:** Flick (or drag to the ledge), Accept.

9. **Write a note.**
   **What you do:** Drag the blank note from the shelf onto the Medici, then type "center of every path" on the keyboard that appears in the scene, or dictate it where the browser allows.
   **What you see:** The first guess is "a note on this one", previewed attached to the Medici; Next guess would offer "the same note on the Medici's in-laws". The keyboard's Done key is Accept. The note becomes a row in the rule list.
   **Controls:** Drag a material, in-scene keyboard or dictation, Accept.

10. **Undo a mistake.**
    **What you do:**
    - Before accepting: you pinch-drag the Medici a little by accident and let go inside the graph. The guess "move just this node" previews the Medici in their new place. Tap No and they go back. If you simply carry on, the preview is dropped when you start the next demonstration; it is never applied.
    - After accepting: you pinch tap the filter row to make it current and flick Barbadori to tighten the filter. You accept the guess without reading it, then notice that Salviati has gone to the ledge too. You drag Salviati up off the ledge.
      **What you see:** The guess you accepted was [fewer than 3 marriages], which hid both Barbadori and Salviati. Dragging Salviati up makes it re-infer: [PageRank] [below 0.055], with the reason "keeps Salviati (0.061), still hides Barbadori (0.050)". The preview shows Salviati coming back and nothing else changing. You Accept, and the new rule replaces the old row in the list. To undo the tightening as a whole instead, tap No with nothing pending: the button reads "Take back: hide fewer than 3 marriages", the preview shows Barbadori and Salviati returning, and Accept confirms. Tapping No again with nothing pending offers the change before that.
      **Controls:** No; pinch tap a row; flick; pinch-drag off the ledge; Take back; Accept.

11. **Save.**
    **What you do:** There is nothing to demonstrate: every Accept is already saved. Pinch tap the project chip at the strip's left end. Its verb chip lists rename, save a copy, export and settings; pick rename, type "Medici study", and Accept. To keep the whole method for another graph, you would have tapped Record before step 5 and again after step 8.
    **What you see:** The project chip shows the new name and "saved". The rule list is the project's history of layers, filters, runs, selections and notes.
    **Controls:** Pinch tap, tap a chip, in-scene keyboard or dictation, Accept.

### Advanced journeys

- **Compound rule.** Pinch tap Strozzi and Guadagni, then flick the Medici. The guess is [families] [PageRank above 0.08] [and] [not married to the Medici], with the reason "both examples are high and outside the Medici marriages; the counter-example is high and inside". Because a selection guess was pending, the Medici are only crossed out in the tray, not hidden. Next guess offers the [or] form. To drop a clause, flick its chip off the strip.
- **Tuning algorithm options.** Pinch tap the PageRank row, then Strozzi and Guadagni, and drag Strozzi's thumbnail above Guadagni's to show you disagree with the order. The guesses are "PageRank with damping 0.6 puts Strozzi above Guadagni", "betweenness" and "closeness". The search over options is honest about failure: when no setting fits, the strip says "no PageRank setting orders these this way; closest: damping 0.5". When you already know what you want, tap the PageRank chip to see its options as value chips.
- **Comparing two runs.** Pinch tap two run rows in the rule list (PageRank and betweenness). The guesses are "compare ranks", "difference" and "where they disagree". The preview lifts each family by how much its rank changed and outlines the biggest movers, with the reason "the Medici and Guadagni are 1st and 2nd by both; Salviati is 10th by PageRank and 4th by betweenness".
- **Layers on subsets.** Pinch tap the "married to the Medici" selection row, then drop a swatch on two of its members. Because both examples are inside that selection, the first guess's scope chip reads [in: families married to the Medici]; tap the chip to change it to [in: whole graph]. The layer touches only its scope.
- **Recipes.** Tap Record, do steps 5, 7 and 8, tap Record again. The tape lands on the shelf with one slot, "the graph". On another dataset, drag the tape onto the graph. Each recorded guess replays as a pending guess with its preview, so you accept or correct each step instead of trusting the whole recipe blind.
- **Joining tables.** This stays on the 2D page. The natural extension is a Flash Fill for keys: drag one table row onto the node it describes, and the system guesses the key column and the match. That needs a table view in VR, which this prototype does not have.

### How it scales

- **60+ algorithm catalog.** You never browse the catalog first. A demonstration shows the outcome you expect (an order, a size, a group), and the system ranks the algorithms whose results agree with it, previewing at most three. Cheap measures run for the preview. Expensive ones appear by name with their cost estimate and run only when you pick them. A chip's alternatives list is searchable, so a known algorithm is two taps away.
- **New object types.** Anything you can pinch can be an example: a node, an edge, a group, a run, a style layer row, a filter row, a note, the project. Inference needs only that the object type describes its attributes, relations and verbs; the strip then guesses over those, and the verb chip lists the verbs.
- **Plugins.** A plugin algorithm that returns a value per node or edge automatically becomes a candidate for ranking, sizing, coloring and filtering guesses. A plugin can add a reason template (a code with parameters) so its guesses explain themselves.
- **The limit.** The limit is the set of rules the system can guess. Show Me works for thresholds, conjunctions, structural relations (neighbors, paths, communities) and measures with a visible effect on each element. It runs out in three places:
    - It cannot infer verbs that have no visible effect on an example: export, settings, save a copy. Those are reached by tapping the object (often the project chip) and picking from its verb chip, which is a short list, not a menu tree.
    - It needs more examples when many measures agree; centrality measures are highly correlated on small graphs.
    - On a 10,000-node graph, two examples tell it almost nothing, so the borderline list and counter-examples do most of the work.

### On each device

| Device            | Inputs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Meta Quest 3 / 3S | Hands (WebXR hand tracking, 25 joints per hand), Touch controllers                                                                                                                                                                                           | Point uses the system hand ray, so hands can rest low. Pinch tap, pinch-drag and two-hand pull come from the standard select events; flick speed comes from the hand joints, with a threshold tuned against tracking jitter, and drag-to-ledge covers the cases where a fast throw loses tracking. No gesture uses a palm turned toward you, so nothing collides with the system menu gesture. No eye tracking is exposed to WebXR. Speech recognition in Quest Browser is not assumed; the in-scene keyboard is the fallback for text. Controllers give the most precise flick and drag. |
| Samsung Galaxy XR | Hands (WebXR hand tracking in Chrome on Android XR), controllers where paired                                                                                                                                                                                | Same mapping as Quest. The device has eye tracking, but this design does not assume Chrome exposes gaze to WebXR, so you aim with the hand ray. Dictation may work where Chrome offers speech recognition; the in-scene keyboard always works.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Apple Vision Pro  | Eyes and hands (Safari WebXR transient-pointer: gaze plus pinch gives a ray only from the moment of the pinch, then follows the hand); hand joints with the hand-tracking feature; PS VR2 Sense controllers where Safari exposes them as WebXR input sources | Gaze is not exposed before the pinch, so there is no hover: the outline and name appear when you pinch, and reading a value is a pinch tap that adds it to the tray (step 6). Pinch tap, Accept and pinch-drag feel native. Two-hand pull uses two simultaneous transient pointers. Flicks use hand-joint speed when hand tracking is granted, otherwise drag-to-ledge. Text comes from the in-scene keyboard, or dictation where Safari allows it.                                                                                                                                       |

### Why it should work

- **Flash Fill** (Excel 2013, built on Gulwani's program synthesis from examples) showed that ordinary users accept a rule inferred from one or two examples and fix it with one more. It is one of the most used inference features in shipped software.
- **Trifacta Wrangler and "predictive interaction"** (Kandel et al., CHI 2011; Heer, Hellerstein and Kandel, CIDR 2015) showed that this loop works for data transformation: select an example, see ranked suggestions with previews, pick one. The preview is what earns the user's trust.
- **Tableau's "keep only" and "exclude", and Gmail's "filter messages like these",** show that many analysts filter by acting on examples, not by writing a condition.
- **Pygmalion (Smith 1975) and Eager (Cypher 1991)** established demonstrate-then-generalize. Eager's lesson -- highlight the prediction and let the user keep going or confirm -- is exactly the pending-guess preview.
- **Pinch is the native select gesture on all three headsets,** so the core loop needs no new gesture. The less common inputs map to physical meanings people already know: a flick throws something away, a two-hand pull makes it bigger, putting one card above another ranks it.
- **The 60+ catalog sits behind "show me the outcome".** That removes the hardest part of VR menus: reading long lists at arm's length.

### What it would teach us

- How many examples people give before the first guess is right, and whether that number is small enough for work done in the air.
- Whether people read the reason line or accept on the preview alone (automation bias), and whether naming borderline cases changes that.
- Whether ordering two thumbnails in the tray reads as "this ranks above that" without being taught, and whether that can replace running an algorithm by name.
- Whether flicking counter-examples feels natural and precise in the air, or whether people fall back to dragging onto the ledge.
- Whether people reach for Take back or for correcting by counter-example after accepting, and which they trust.
- Whether views that apply live while every other guess waits for Accept feels consistent or confusing.
- Whether recorded tapes are reused, and whether replaying them as pending guesses is trusted more than a recipe that runs in one go.
- Which verbs resist demonstration (save, export, settings), and how often people need the verb chip.

### Known risks

- **Wrong guesses accepted without reading.** Mitigation: the count and the borderline names sit next to Accept. When a guess changes many more elements than the examples imply, its count is shown larger. Take back is always one tap plus Accept, and correcting by counter-example is one drag.
- **Ambiguity: many rules fit two examples.** Mitigation: prefer the simplest rule, and say in the reason line what else fits ("also fits: fewer than 2 marriages"). Next guess cycles the alternatives, and the chips are editable when you know exactly what you mean.
- **Experts want to name the verb.** Mitigation: tap the thing, then the verb chip lists every verb the object has, with search; a known algorithm takes one tap on the thing and two on chips, never a hunt.
- **Inference latency on large graphs and big catalogs.** Mitigation: preview only the current guess and name the next two, run cheap measures first, and show expensive candidates with their cost estimate, running them only on request.
- **Re-inference after accepting changes more than intended.** Mitigation: the re-inferred rule is never applied automatically. It is a new pending guess with its own preview and a diff ("Salviati back, nothing else changes").
- **"Put it here" is overloaded.** Mitigation: it has one meaning, and where the thing lands says why, by fixed regions: the tray (order), outside the graph toward you (view), inside the graph (move the node), the ledge (out). When a drag is ambiguous, the first guess is the most literal one, and No costs nothing.
- **Tap, drag, flick and pull start the same way.** Mitigation: fixed thresholds, each tunable: under about 2 cm of movement is a tap, a fast release away from you or down is a flick, a slow release is a drop, and a second pinch during a drag makes it a pull.
- **Flick misfires from tracking noise, and fast throws lose hand tracking.** Mitigation: a speed threshold, drag-to-ledge as the slow equivalent, and nothing is hidden before Accept; a stray counter-example is one pinch tap to drop from the tray.
- **Small targets at 75 cm.** Mitigation: the ray snaps to the nearest node within a few degrees, and the tray thumbnails are large, so ordering and sizing happen on the thumbnails, not on the tiny nodes.
- **Arm fatigue.** Mitigation: system rays work with hands resting, the strip is low and close, and no input needs held arms or a held pose.
- **Hidden things are out of reach for correction.** Mitigation: the set-aside ledge holds them, borderline first. On a large graph it shows a sample of the borderline ones and a count.
- **Vision Pro has no hover.** Mitigation: the outline appears when you pinch, values are read by tapping into the tray, and a pinch tap on the wrong thing is undone by tapping it again.

### Comparison row

| #   | Name    | One-line idea                                                                                                                      | Mode model                                                                                        | Navigation                                                                                                                            | Selection                                                                     | Command surface                                                                                                          | Parameters                                                                        | Undo                                                                                           |
| --- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 19  | Show Me | Do it by hand to one or two examples; the system guesses the rule, previews it on everything, you correct it with counter-examples | demonstrate, generalize, confirm; the verb chip lists an object's verbs when you want to name one | by example with "put it here": drag a node out toward you = bring to front, two examples pulled apart = frame these; views apply live | by example: pinch two, "nodes like these" with reason; flick counter-examples | one low rule strip: example tray, editable chips, Accept / Next guess / No, materials and tapes shelf, Record, rule list | inferred from examples; reorder or resize tray thumbnails; chips for exact values | No before accepting; Take back after; or correct with a counter-example and the rule re-infers |

## Prototype 20: Pilot and Navigator

Two people with split jobs: the driver drives the view and points at objects, the navigator holds that object's verbs as tokens and hands one across; the driver pulling it free is the second key.

### How it differs, layer by layer

| Layer           | Pilot and Navigator                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Differs from                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Mode model      | Split authority across two people. A command runs only when the navigator hands a verb across and the driver pulls it free: the offer is the first key, the driver's pull is the second, and only the driver's pull commits. Either can refuse. Undo is deliberately NOT two-key: either person undoes the last step alone, in one move, and the undone step goes back to the navigator to offer again.                                                                                                       | Every one of the 11 is single-user. Select-then-command (1, 2, 3), tools (4, 6, 8), signs (7), casting (9), lock-on (10) and context menus (11) all let one person both choose and commit.                                                                        |
| Navigation      | Driver only: close a hand on the graph (by ray) to move it, both hands to turn and scale it. The graph moves as an object for both people; nobody's head or viewpoint is ever moved by the other. The navigator can turn a private palm-sized copy (a Peek token) and offer it to call the driver to that view.                                                                                                                                                                                               | Two-hand grab (1, 3) and grips (11) belong to the only user; Workbench's miniature (5) and HUD radar (10) steer your own view. Here one person steers for both, and the other can only propose a view.                                                            |
| Selection       | The driver's pointer is the shared selection and picks the object. The navigator never points at nodes; she refines only by rules (neighbors, attribute ranges, top N), which are verbs and go through the handoff like any other.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Point and pinch (1), "this" (2), lasso (3, 11), painting (4), tongs (6), lock-on (10): all made by the person who also commands.                                                                                                                                  |
| Command surface | No panel. Every verb is a small object (a token) in the navigator's kit. The kit is object-first: its front row always holds the verbs of whatever the driver is pointing at, and tokens that cannot land on that target are dimmed. She picks one up and holds it out; the driver takes it with the off hand and it lands on the driver's target. Nothing on the driver's side is pressable.                                                                                                                 | Palettes (1), command bars (2, 5), marking menus (3), belts and racks (4), pegboard (6), glyphs and runes (9), edge strips (10), menus at the object (11). The closest are Tool Belt (4) and Workshop (6), where one user fires their own tools from fixed slots. |
| Parameters      | The navigator sets the value on the token (twist its ring) before offering it. During the joint hold only the driver's twist changes the value, so the two never fight over a ring; the ghost at the target updates as it turns. Pull a token open to reach its other options.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Sliders (1), spoken numbers (2), wrist dial (3), stick dial and trigger pressure (4), scrubbing (5), knobs (6, 8), finger counts (7). Here a value is set by one person and adjusted by another before commit.                                                    |
| Feedback        | A verb in transit is visible to both. It glows while both hands hold it, and a ghost of its effect with a count shows on the driver's target. A refused token flies back to the navigator. Spent tokens hang on a string from the driver's off wrist, in order, as the visible history; the navigator has a mirrored copy of the same string along the near edge of her kit. The navigator has a private readout of numbers that the driver does not see; she says them aloud or holds a line out to show it. | No existing prototype shows a pending command to a second person, and none splits what each person can see.                                                                                                                                                       |

**Setup.** Fixed environment: a solid colored background in full VR, the graph at chest height 70-80 cm in front of the driver. Two headsets, any mix of Meta Quest, Samsung Galaxy XR and Apple Vision Pro, joined to one shared session over the network. Remote by default: each person stands or sits in their own room and sees the other as head and hands, the navigator placed at the driver's off-hand side and slightly forward, at the near corner of the graph, so the driver sees her and the graph in one glance. The pass (where tokens change hands) is anchored to each person's own body, a forearm's length in front of their off-hand hip, at the lower edge of their view; the other person's avatar hand is drawn arriving there. Nobody reaches behind or across their body. Co-located is optional (one room, a one-time alignment step, and then the pass is a real shared point between them). Driver: hands or controllers. Navigator: hands or controllers, or a laptop browser. A voice channel between the two people is always open (that is talking, not a command input). Dictation into a note token is optional. Both roles work seated.

### The control vocabulary

The same input means the same thing for both people. Closing the hand always means "hold whatever is under it"; opening it always means "let go"; twisting always means "change the value under your fingers"; only the driver's pull ever commits.

| Input                                                         | Who                                    | Meaning everywhere                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Point (hand ray or controller ray)                            | Driver                                 | Aim. What the ray hits is the target; the kit's front row shows that target's verbs, and every ghost and every commit lands there. Hovered nodes show their label and, after a run, their value.                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Pinch / trigger click                                         | Driver                                 | Toggle the pointed element in the shared selection. On empty space: clear the selection (a cleared selection of more than one element is kept as a light step on the string, so undo brings it back). On a spent token: lift it out of the string to float beside the pass as the target (see "Where a verb lands").                                                                                                        |
| Pinch / trigger held and swept                                | Driver                                 | Add every element the ray hits first along the sweep to the selection.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Close the hand (fist) / grip                                  | Both                                   | Hold what is under the hand. Navigator: on a token in the kit, pick it up. Driver: on a token held out at the pass, take it; with the ray on the graph, move it (one hand) or turn and scale it (two hands); on the top spent token of the string, pull it off (undo); on the knot at the wrist, hold the whole string (to swap roles). Navigator on her mirrored string: copy a step (or, with two hands, a run of steps). |
| Open the hand                                                 | Both                                   | Let go. A token nobody holds returns to the navigator's kit. If the navigator lets go during a joint hold, the token is now the driver's alone and still pending: nothing runs until the driver pulls. If the driver lets go, the token is refused and returns to the navigator.                                                                                                                                            |
| Pull toward yourself, past about 20 cm from where you took it | Driver commits; navigator withdraws    | The driver pulling a held token commits it, whether or not the navigator still holds it. The navigator pulling it back withdraws it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Twist the ring you hold                                       | Both (driver only during a joint hold) | Change that ring's value. Detents for steps. A slow twist moves one step per detent, a fast twist moves many; holding the wrist turned past about 60 degrees keeps the ring turning, so no re-grip is needed for a long range.                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Pull a token's two ends apart                                 | Navigator                              | Open it: it lengthens into a rod with one ring per option. Push the ends together to close it. One-handed: pinch an end and draw it out.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Touch two tokens end to end                                   | Navigator                              | Snap them into one compound token (AND). A rule token's "not" ring flips it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Hold a note token to the mouth                                | Navigator                              | Dictate the note's text (content, not a command). Without speech recognition, a pinch keyboard opens on the token.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Hold a readout line out at the pass                           | Navigator                              | Show it to the driver as a text card while she holds it. Changes nothing; not a command.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Pull the top spent token off the string and open the hand     | Driver (or the navigator on her copy)  | Undo that step, alone, at once. The undone token goes to the Return slot at the front of the kit with its values intact, where the navigator can offer it again (redo, with or without changes).                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Hold the string's knot out at the pass                        | Driver                                 | Swap roles: when the navigator takes the knot, she becomes driver and the kit moves to the other person.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

Controllers: grip is "close the hand", trigger is the driver's pinch, wrist roll while holding the grip is "twist". Thumbsticks and face buttons are unused, so nothing can fire without the handoff.

### The handoff, state by state

| State              | How you get there                                                                                   | What both see                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| In the kit         | Navigator has not picked it up                                                                      | Token in its place in the kit; the driver sees the kit only as shapes at the navigator's waist. Tokens that cannot land on the driver's current target are dimmed.                                                                                                  |
| Offered            | Navigator holds it out at the pass                                                                  | Token outlined in the navigator's color, its main value written on its ring; if it is a rule, its matches show faintly on the graph for both.                                                                                                                       |
| Joint hold         | Driver closes the off hand on it                                                                    | Token glows; a ghost of its effect sits on the driver's target with a count ("will hide 7 families", "will run on 15 nodes, instant"). Pointing elsewhere moves the ghost. An invalid target dims the glow and cannot commit. Controllers pulse once on both sides. |
| Driver holds alone | Navigator opens her hand during the joint hold                                                      | Same ghost and glow, outline in the driver's color. Still pending.                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Committed          | Driver pulls it free past about 20 cm, after the ghost has shown on that target for at least 300 ms | The effect lands on the target once the server confirms; the glow holds until then. The token shrinks and hangs on the driver's string (and on the navigator's copy).                                                                                               |
| Refused            | Driver opens the hand, the navigator pulls it back, or the driver pulls before the 300 ms           | Token flies back to the navigator's hand with its values intact, so she can change it and offer again.                                                                                                                                                              |
| Tug                | Both pull at once                                                                                   | Refusal wins: the token returns to the navigator.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

**Target lock.** The moment the driver's off hand starts the pull, the target freezes on whatever the pointer rested on for the previous 300 ms, so the small sympathetic movement of the pointing hand during the pull cannot slide the commit onto a neighbor. To re-aim, the driver stops pulling, points elsewhere, and pulls again.

### Where a verb lands

| The driver's pointer is on                                                                                                             | The verb applies to                                                    |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Empty space                                                                                                                            | The whole graph                                                        |
| A selected element                                                                                                                     | The whole selection                                                    |
| An unselected node or edge                                                                                                             | That one element                                                       |
| A note                                                                                                                                 | That note                                                              |
| A spent token the driver has pinched out of the string (it floats beside the pass until used, or until the driver pinches empty space) | That history object: the run, style layer, filter step or note it made |

### The kit

A shallow open tray at the navigator's waist (on her lap when seated), tilted up toward her, that follows her. Nothing in it is pressable; tokens can only be picked up. Rows never move, so the navigator learns where things are; only the front row changes, and it changes with what the driver points at.

| Row                        | Tokens                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| -------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| This (front row)           | The verbs of the driver's current target, from its object type. Node: Neighbors, Focus, Note, Hide, Group. Edge: Hide, Note, Weight by. Selection: Group, Hide, Color by, Neighbors. Run: Re-run, Compare, Color by, Size by, Remove. Style layer: Edit, Move up, Move down, Hide, Remove. Filter step: Edit, Turn off, Remove. Note: Edit, Move, Remove. Whole graph: Frame, Find, Save. Plus the Return slot: the most recently undone token. |
| Look                       | Frame (fit to target), Focus (center on target), Peek (a palm-sized private copy of the graph she can turn; offering it moves the shared graph to that view), saved Peek tokens                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Select (rules)             | Neighbors (ring: hops), Find (ring: names in order), Top N (ring: N), Where (ring: threshold), Same group, Invert                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Analyze                    | The six most recently used algorithms, then the Catalog drawer: one compartment per family (centrality, community, paths, clustering, flow, link prediction, layout, ...)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Style                      | Color by, Size by, Label by, Hide                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Filter                     | Keep where, Remove where                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Record                     | Note, Group, Compare, Save                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| History (navigator's copy) | A mirror of the driver's string along the near edge, so she can copy steps without reaching for the driver's wrist                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Readout (navigator only)   | A text strip on the tray's near edge: the driver's target, its attributes, the latest run's top values, counts. Not shown to the driver unless she holds a line out.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

A closed token shows one ring, its main value. Its other options default to the most recent result or the target's main attribute; pull it open to set them. A destructive verb (Hide, Remove, Remove where, Keep where) always shows its ghost as what will go, with a count, before it can commit.

### The journey

**1. Enter**

- **What you do:** On the 2D page, one person opens the Florentine families and shares the session link; the other joins. Each picks a role on the join page and presses Enter VR.
- **What you see:** The graph at chest height in front of the driver. The navigator sees the same graph from the driver's off-hand side, her kit at her waist, and the driver's head, hands and ray. The driver sees the navigator's head and hands and an empty string at the off wrist.
- **Controls:** None in VR. Roles can be swapped later by handing the string's knot across.

**2. Get oriented**

- **What you do:** The driver closes both hands on the graph, turns it and pulls it a little closer. The navigator reads the readout aloud: "fifteen families, twenty marriages".
- **What you see:** The graph turns for both, as an object in front of a still background; neither person's viewpoint moves.
- **Controls:** Driver: point at the graph and close both hands. Navigator: none.

**3. Find the Medici**

- **What you do:** The driver points at empty space; the front row shows the whole graph's verbs. The navigator picks up Find and twists its ring to "Medici"; the matching node glows faintly for both. She holds it out. The driver takes it and pulls.
- **What you see:** During the joint hold the ghost rings the Medici node with "1 match"; on commit the view frames it and the Medici become the selection. (If the driver had spotted the label first, a pinch on it would select it directly; the navigator never points.)
- **Controls:** Navigator: close, twist, hold out. Driver: point at empty space, close the off hand, pull.

**4. Who they married into**

- **What you do:** The driver points at the Medici (selected); Neighbors appears in the front row. The navigator picks it up (ring at 1 hop) and holds it out. The driver takes it.
- **What you see:** The ghost outlines six families (Acciaiuoli, Albizzi, Barbadori, Ridolfi, Salviati, Tornabuoni) with "+6". On the pull they join the selection; the spent Neighbors token hangs on the driver's string.
- **Controls:** Same handoff. Twisting the ring to 2 during the joint hold would have grown the ghost before commit.

**5. Who matters most (PageRank)**

- **What you do:** While the driver looks around, the navigator opens the Catalog drawer, the centrality compartment, picks up PageRank (ring: damping 0.85) and offers it. The driver points at empty space and takes it.
- **What you see:** Joint hold shows "will run on 15 nodes, instant". On commit the run finishes; the readout lists the top families. The navigator says "Medici first, about 0.15, then Guadagni and Strozzi".
- **Controls:** Navigator: close on the drawer, close on PageRank, hold out. Driver: point, take, pull.

**6. Read the result**

- **What you do:** The driver sweeps the pointer across the graph. The navigator reads the ranked list and calls out anything the driver passes, or holds the top-five line out at the pass so the driver can read it.
- **What you see:** Each hovered family shows its score at the pointer. The navigator's readout shows the full ranking. To pull the top five into the selection, the navigator offers Top N (ring: 5) and the driver takes it at empty space.
- **Controls:** Driver: point. Navigator: optionally hold a readout line out. Optional Top N handoff.

**7. Color and size by PageRank**

- **What you do:** The navigator picks up Color by (its attribute defaults to the latest run, PageRank) in one hand and Size by in the other. She offers Color by; the driver, pointing at empty space, takes it and pulls. Then Size by.
- **What you see:** The ghost recolors the whole graph during each joint hold; each commit adds a style layer, and each layer is a spent token on the string.
- **Controls:** Two handoffs. The driver twists Size by's ring (largest size) while holding, to make the Medici less dominant, then pulls.

**8. Keep only strong families (filter)**

- **What you do:** The navigator twists Keep where's ring to 0.07 (attribute: PageRank, by default) and offers it. The driver takes it at empty space, sees too few left, twists the ring down a notch while still holding, then pulls.
- **What you see:** The ghost fades the families that would go and shows the count that stays; the count updates as the ring turns. On commit the faded families leave and a filter step hangs on the string.
- **Controls:** Handoff with an adjusted value: the navigator set the first value, the driver adjusted it before commit.

**9. Write a note**

- **What you do:** The navigator holds a Note token to her mouth and says "Medici: center of the marriage network" (or types it on the pinch keyboard), then offers it. The driver points at what he believes is the Medici and takes it.
- **What you see:** The text is written on the token during the hold, and the ghost shows the note pinned to the pointed node with that node's name. On commit it pins to the node.
- **Controls:** Navigator: hold to mouth (or pinch keyboard), hold out. Driver: point, take, pull.

**10. Undo a mistake**

- **What you do:** The note landed on Tornabuoni, which sits beside the Medici: the driver pulled without reading the ghost's name. The driver closes the dominant hand on the top token of the string (the note), pulls it off and opens the hand. The navigator picks the note out of the Return slot, offers it again, and the driver points at the Medici, checks the ghost and pulls.
- **What you see:** The moment the driver lets go, the note lifts off Tornabuoni and drops into the Return slot with its text intact. Deeper undo takes tokens off the top in order; the string shows how many steps back you are. The navigator can undo the same way from her copy of the string if the driver's hands are busy.
- **Controls:** Driver: close on the string's top token, pull, open. Navigator: take from the Return slot, hold out. Driver: point, take, pull.

**11. Save**

- **What you do:** The navigator offers Save; the driver points at empty space and takes it.
- **What you see:** The ghost shows the project name; on commit the readout shows "saved" and the string's tokens dim slightly to mark the saved point.
- **Controls:** One handoff.

### Advanced journeys

- **Compound rule:** The navigator opens Where (attribute PageRank, at least 0.07), snaps it end to end to Neighbors (1 hop), and offers the compound token. The driver takes it pointing at the Medici: "strong families married to the Medici" becomes the selection. The faint match preview lets the navigator check the rule before offering.
- **Tuning algorithm options:** The navigator pulls an algorithm token open; it lengthens into one ring per option (PageRank: damping, iterations, tolerance, weight attribute). She sets them and closes it. Only the main ring stays visible to the driver, who can twist it during the joint hold.
- **Comparing two runs:** The navigator closes her hand on the PageRank step in her copy of the string to get a copy of the token, sets its damping to 0.5 and offers it; the driver takes it, and there are now two runs. Then she offers Compare (its two rings pick the runs); the driver takes it at empty space and the graph shows the difference per node, with the biggest movers in the readout.
- **Layers on subsets:** The driver selects the Medici and their neighbors, then takes Color by while pointing at a selected node: the layer applies to the selection only. To change it later, the driver pinches that layer's spent token so it floats beside the pass; the front row now shows the layer's verbs (Edit, Move up, Move down, Hide, Remove), and the navigator offers Edit with new values. The ghost shows the layer's new look before the pull.
- **Recipes:** The navigator closes one hand on the first and the other on the last of a run of steps in her copy of the string and pulls; a copy of those steps becomes one bundled token, which goes into her kit. Offered later on another graph, it replays all its steps at the driver's target, ghosting the final result first; it lands as one step on the string, so one undo removes it.
- **Joining tables:** Stays on the 2D page. Neither role has a good way to match columns by handing objects.

### How it scales

- **60+ algorithm catalog:** The Catalog drawer has one compartment per family; the six most recent sit in the Analyze row. Browsing is the navigator's job and happens while the driver keeps exploring, so the catalog's depth costs the driver nothing. Where speech recognition exists, saying a name into the open drawer lifts the matching token.
- **New object types:** A new object type brings its verbs (they fill the front row when the driver points at one) and a rule for what the pointer can land on; the five landing rules above stay the same. Verbs on history objects (re-run, edit a layer, remove a filter step) land on a spent token the driver has pinched out of the string.
- **Plugins:** A plugin registers tokens: a name, its rings (its options, with defaults), the object types it applies to, and a preview. They appear in the drawer's family compartment and in the front row for those object types. Nothing about the handoff changes.
- **The limit:** Every command costs one handoff, about 1.5 to 3 seconds and two people's attention. It suits deliberate analysis and teaching; it is slow for fast exploratory sweeps, which stay with the driver's pointer and selection. The kit's fixed rows hold about two dozen tokens before the navigator stops finding them by place, and the string grows long after 30 or so steps (older tokens coil up and are reached by count, not by eye).

### On each device

| Device                  | Driver                                                                                                                                  | Navigator                                                                                    | What WebXR exposes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ----------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Meta Quest 3 / 3S       | Hand ray or Touch controller ray; pinch or trigger; fist or grip to hold                                                                | Hands or controllers; fist or grip to pick up, wrist roll to twist                           | Hands (with joint poses) and controllers in Meta Quest Browser. Pinch arrives as the browser's select event; the fist and the wrist twist are computed by the page from joint poses, with hysteresis so a fist is not read as a pinch. Controller haptics through the gamepad's haptic actuator (the joint-hold pulse). No eye tracking for web pages. Speech recognition in the page cannot be assumed, so the pinch keyboard is the default for notes. WebXR has no shared spatial anchors, so co-located use needs a manual alignment step (both touch the same physical spot). |
| Samsung Galaxy XR       | Hand ray or controller ray                                                                                                              | Hands or controllers                                                                         | WebXR in Chrome on Android XR with hand joints and controllers; fist and twist computed from joints as on Quest. Eye tracking is used by the system, not exposed to web pages. Same co-location limit.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Apple Vision Pro        | Hand ray computed from the tracked hand (shoulder to wrist) for both hover and pinch, so what is hovered is always what a pinch selects | Hands only: picking up, twisting and holding out tokens are direct, which suits the platform | Safari WebXR gives tracked hand joints and a transient pointer that carries the gaze direction only at the moment of a pinch. The page uses the pinch as an event but ignores the transient pointer's gaze target, so one pinch never has two possible targets and hover (impossible by gaze) and selection agree.
