Game controllers only where Safari exposes them as WebXR gamepads; otherwise hands. No haptics with hands, so the joint hold is shown by glow only.                                                                                                             |
| Laptop (navigator only) | --                                                                                                                                      | The kit is a page; dragging a token onto the pass offers it, dragging it back withdraws it   | Plain web page, no WebXR. The driver's side is unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Mixed pair              | Any of the above                                                                                                                        | Any of the above                                                                             | The session is synced over the network by graphty's own server (WebXR has no multi-user layer): hand poses at about 30 Hz with interpolation, token ownership and commits decided on the server, voice over WebRTC. A Quest driver and a Vision Pro navigator work together.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

### Why it should work

- The surgical instrument pass and the rally co-driver are trained divisions of labor that keep the operator's eyes on the work while someone else prepares the next tool. The pass itself has a protocol: the receiver's grip is felt before the giver lets go. This prototype copies that protocol, with the receiver's pull as the commit.
- Two-person rules (launch keys, bank vaults, change review in software) exist because a second deliberate act catches single-person slips. Here the second act is the physical pull, with the effect previewed on the target at the moment of taking. Undo is kept one-person on purpose, because a safety net that needs two people is not always there.
- Pair programming studies (Williams, Kessler and others, around 2000) found pairs produced fewer defects for modestly more person-time; one person steering while the other plans ahead is the same split.
- Pairs analyzing networks in head-mounted displays did as well as or better than in a CAVE (Cordeil and others, "Immersive collaborative analysis of network connectivity", IEEE TVCG 2017), so co-analysis of graphs in headsets is not a stretch.
- Handing objects between avatars is something social VR users do without instruction (passing items in Rec Room or VRChat), so the handoff needs little teaching.
- Removing every command surface from the driver leaves them with three inputs (point, pinch, hold), which removes mode errors on that side; Spaceteam and Keep Talking and Nobody Explodes show that splitting what each person can see drives useful talk.

### What it would teach us

- Does the pull catch mistakes, or do pairs rubber-stamp (the driver pulls without reading the ghost)?
- What does a handoff cost per command, and does it fall as the navigator learns to anticipate the next verb from the front row?
- Is the navigator's private readout good for the pair, or does the driver feel blind?
- Does letting the driver adjust a value during the joint hold help, or does the navigator feel overruled?
- Does the handoff feel solid remotely, with only avatar hands, a body-anchored pass and network delay, or does it need co-location?
- Is the value in the second person or in the two-key structure? If a solo user would want a self-handoff, the structure is worth borrowing for destructive verbs elsewhere.
- Can the kit's fixed rows plus a drawer reach a 60-algorithm catalog without the driver waiting?
- Is a hanging string a readable history and a usable undo?

### Known risks

- **Needs two people and two headsets.** Mitigation: the navigator can join from a laptop browser, where the kit is a page and the handoff is dragging a token onto the pass. If the navigator drops out mid-session, the kit moves to the driver's waist and the driver hands tokens from one hand to the other (the same states, one person). Solo users use another prototype.
- **Rubber-stamping.** Mitigation: a commit needs the ghost to have been on that target for at least 300 ms; a token pulled faster returns to the navigator. The ghost always names the target and counts what changes.
- **Pointer slip at commit.** Mitigation: the target lock above freezes the target when the pull starts. Aiming at the wrong neighbor (journey step 10) is still possible; the ghost names the target, and one-person undo covers the rest.
- **Ownership conflicts over the network.** Mitigation: the server owns who holds each token; tugs resolve as refusals; a commit is shown only after the server confirms it, with the glow held until then.
- **Undo blocked by the other person.** Mitigation: undo needs no handoff; either person undoes from their own string, and redo is an ordinary offer from the Return slot.
- **Physical collisions when co-located.** Mitigation: remote is the default; co-located pairs see each other's full avatar and stand on fixed marks with the pass between them.
- **Arm and neck fatigue.** Mitigation: the pass is anchored to each person's own hip, forward and low, and the reach is short; the driver points with the dominant hand resting low; fine values come from slow twists, not from holding the arm out; the kit tilts up so the navigator does not bend her neck far.
- **Motion sickness.** Mitigation: only the graph moves; neither person's viewpoint is ever moved by the other, and a called view (Peek) eases the graph into place as an object.
- **One-handed use.** Mitigation: the driver can point, rest 300 ms to lock the target (it is outlined), then take and pull with the same hand; the navigator opens tokens by pinching one end and drawing it out.
- **Role imbalance or boredom.** Mitigation: roles swap by handing the string's knot across, at any time, in one move.
- **Hand-tracking grasp of small tokens.** Mitigation: tokens are fist-sized at the pass and snap into a closing hand within 5 cm.
- **Speech-dependent steps.** Mitigation: the pinch keyboard covers dictation; readout lines can be held out as text for a driver who cannot hear the navigator well.

### Comparison row

| #   | Name                | One-line idea                                                                                                                                                    | Mode model                                                        | Navigation                                                                     | Selection                                    | Command surface                                                                                 | Parameters                                                                    | Undo                                                                                                          |
| --- | ------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 20  | Pilot and Navigator | Two people: the driver drives and points at objects, the navigator holds that object's verbs as tokens and hands one across; the driver's pull is the second key | split authority, two-key handoff (only the driver's pull commits) | driver only, by grabbing the graph; navigator proposes views with a Peek token | driver's pointer; navigator refines by rules | navigator's kit of verb tokens, front row = the pointed object's verbs, passed across; no panel | rings set by the navigator, adjusted only by the driver during the joint hold | refuse before commit; either person pulls the top spent token off the string alone; redo by offering it again |

## Prototype 21: Mixing Desk

A mixing console with one channel strip per style layer, filter and selection: nothing is ever applied or committed, every effect is a live fader, two algorithm runs sit on two decks with a crossfader between them, and you can hear any channel.

### How it differs, layer by layer

| Layer                   | Mixing Desk                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Differs from                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ----------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Mode model              | Live mix with no apply step. Every style layer, filter and selection is an always-on channel strip; its fader sets how much of it reaches the graph. "Committing" is leaving a fader up. Each strip is an object: poke its name to select it, and its verbs and settings appear, full size, in the one selected-channel section, as on a digital console. | Workbench (5) edits properties that then hold; Palette Hand (1), Gesture Grammar (3) and Context Menus (11) pick a command and run it; Physics Lab (15) is live too but commits by freezing time. Here there is no commit at all.         |
| Navigation              | A jog platter on the desk spins the graph's turntable (grab and turn it; let go while turning and the spin carries on; poke it to stop); a nudge strip brings the graph closer or sends it farther; a TILT knob tips it. Four hot cue pads store and recall views. The graph moves, never you, and the hands never touch the graph.                       | Two-hand grab (1, 3), fists (7), palm orbit (9) and the off hand on the stand (8) all handle the graph. Tool Belt (4) and Workshop (6) have turntables, but driven by a stick or by hand on the graph.                                    |
| Selection               | Cue, then send. Aiming at a node or edge and pinching puts it on the cue bus, a ghost preview that is not part of the mix; SEND turns the nodes and edges on cue into their own selection strip, which is mixed like any other strip. Hop expansion is that strip's LOW and HIGH knobs.                                                                   | Every other scheme selects into a transient set. Here a selection is a persistent, mixable strip. HUD (10) accumulates lock-ons, but they drive commands, not a channel.                                                                  |
| Menus / command surface | The console: a row of slim channel strips (name, fader, meter, MUTE, SOLO, CUE), one selected-channel section with the selected strip's source, range, subset, routing and assign buttons, two decks that hold algorithm runs, a crossfader, a library browser with one encoder, and a master section. No menus pop up anywhere.                          | Fixed docks exist in Workbench (5) and Context Menus (11), but as panels of properties; Tool Belt (4) has a cartridge rack. None blends two runs; Cutting Room (23) compares by branching and diffing, this desk compares by crossfading. |
| Parameters              | Faders and knobs, everywhere, with the same feel: grab and move the hand along the desk (knobs too: no wrist twisting). Lifting the hand off the desk while holding enters fine mode. Holding any readout opens a keypad for an exact value. The crossfader blends deck A's result with deck B's.                                                         | Sliders on a palette (1), wrist twist (3), stick dial (4), finger counts (7), in-menu sliders (11). Only here is every value a fader or knob, and only here does a value control blend two results.                                       |
| Feedback                | A level meter on every strip counts the nodes it touches now; cueing a strip or deck plays its values once as a falling run of notes; while a strip is soloed, sweeping your aim ray across the graph (no pinch) plays the value under it as pitch; crossing a threshold clicks once per node. Sound is never the only signal.                            | Sound is feedback in no other prototype. Linked Views (18) shows counts on brushes, but only visually.                                                                                                                                    |
| Undo                    | UNDO and REDO step back and forward through every change to the mix and the project, one per press. Scene pads snapshot and recall the whole desk.                                                                                                                                                                                                        | History lists (5, 11), receipts (6), winding time (9), thumbs down (7). Scene recall restores a whole configuration at once, which none of them do.                                                                                       |

**Setup.** Fixed environment: solid colored background in full VR, the graph floating at chest height 70-80 cm away. The desk floats about 35 cm in front of you, about 15 cm below the elbows, tilted toward you like a real console and about 90 cm wide, below and in front of the graph so it never hides it. It is placed from your head height when you enter, and PLACE on the master section re-seats it: press PLACE, then rest both palms flat for a second on a real table, your lap, or the air where you want it, and the desk snaps to that plane. Resting it on a real table gives the fingers a surface. The desk mirrors for left-handed use, and every control works with either hand: nothing needs two hands. Devices: Meta Quest 3 / 3S and Samsung Galaxy XR with hands (direct touch) or controllers (tip touch and trigger); Apple Vision Pro with hands for the desk and look-and-pinch to aim at the graph. Sound through the headset's speakers or headphones. Voice is optional and used only for TALKBACK (dictating notes).

### The control vocabulary

| Input                                                                                                       | Meaning everywhere                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Hover a fingertip (or controller tip) just above a control                                                  | Show its name and value. Hovering never changes anything.                                                                                                    |
| Poke a button (fingertip pushed in from the front; controller tip touched to it)                            | Press it. Buttons light while on. A brush from the side or from below does not press.                                                                        |
| Pinch a fader, knob, platter or rail and move the hand along the desk (controller: tip on it, hold trigger) | Set its value or position. The value follows the hand; knobs turn as the hand moves sideways.                                                                |
| Lift the hand off the desk while holding                                                                    | Fine mode: 5 cm up is half speed, 10 cm a quarter, 20 cm a tenth. The readout shows the speed.                                                               |
| Hold a readout                                                                                              | Type an exact value on a keypad that slides out of the desk.                                                                                                 |
| Aim at a node or edge and pinch (hand ray; controller ray and trigger; Vision Pro look and pinch)           | Toggle it on the cue bus. The only thing you ever do to the graph itself.                                                                                    |
| Near beats far                                                                                              | A pinch or trigger that starts within 5 cm of a desk control acts on that control and never on the graph, whatever the ray or the eyes point at.             |
| Poke a strip's name (its scribble strip)                                                                    | Select that strip: its settings and verbs fill the selected-channel section.                                                                                 |
| Pinch a strip's name and slide it sideways                                                                  | Move the strip. Strips stack left to right: a strip to the right paints over the ones to its left.                                                           |
| Jog platter: pinch and turn (controller: thumbstick left / right)                                           | Spin the graph's turntable; let go while turning and the spin carries on, slowly.                                                                            |
| Jog platter: poke (controller: click the thumbstick)                                                        | Stop the spin.                                                                                                                                               |
| Nudge strip: pinch and slide (controller: thumbstick up / down)                                             | Bring the graph closer or send it farther. You never move.                                                                                                   |
| TILT knob                                                                                                   | Tip the graph toward or away from you.                                                                                                                       |
| Any pad (hot cue or scene): tap                                                                             | An empty pad stores (a view, or the whole desk); a lit pad recalls it.                                                                                       |
| Any pad: hold until its ring fills (1 second)                                                               | Store over a lit pad. Let go early and nothing happens. UNDO brings back what was overwritten.                                                               |
| Browse encoder: turn                                                                                        | Scroll the library browser.                                                                                                                                  |
| Browse encoder: press                                                                                       | Take the highlighted item: a node goes onto the cue bus; a source or algorithm loads into the armed slot. With no slot armed, ARM blinks and nothing loads.  |
| LOAD (on a deck) or INSERT (in the selected channel): tap                                                   | Arm that slot as the browser's target.                                                                                                                       |
| CUE (strip or deck)                                                                                         | Put it on the cue bus: a ghost preview of it at full strength, whatever its fader says, plus its values played once as notes.                                |
| SEND (master)                                                                                               | Turn the nodes and edges on the cue bus into a new selection strip and clear them from the cue bus. With no nodes or edges on cue, SEND blinks.              |
| SOLO                                                                                                        | Show the graph with only this strip active, all others bypassed, and let your aim ray play its values as pitch.                                              |
| MUTE                                                                                                        | Take a strip out of the mix without losing any setting. A muted strip shows REMOVE in the selected channel; its muted graph is the preview of removing it.   |
| Channel fader                                                                                               | How much of the strip reaches the graph, blended over the strips to its left.                                                                                |
| Crossfader                                                                                                  | Blend deck A's result with deck B's.                                                                                                                         |
| UNDO / REDO (master; controller B or Y is UNDO)                                                             | Step back or forward one change: any control, load, SEND, strip move or removal, pad store, note. View moves and the cue bus are not changes.                |
| TALKBACK: hold / tap                                                                                        | Hold and speak a note / tap to type it. The master display names the note's target first: the last thing put on cue, or the project if the cue bus is empty. |
| BANK buttons                                                                                                | Page the strips: Nodes bank, Edges bank, more strips.                                                                                                        |
| SAVE                                                                                                        | Save the project with every strip, deck, run, scene and note.                                                                                                |
| SOUND                                                                                                       | All desk sound on or off.                                                                                                                                    |
| PLACE, then palms flat                                                                                      | Re-seat the desk on a table, a lap or a spot in the air.                                                                                                     |
| Pinch the desk's front rail and move                                                                        | Move or raise the desk by hand.                                                                                                                              |

### The desk

| Zone                           | Where                                    | What it holds                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Deck A, deck B                 | Back row, left and right                 | One algorithm run each: name, a ranked strip of its result (sorted values, like a DJ waveform; hover along it to read each value), up to four option knobs (a page button for more), a progress ring, CUE, LOAD, SYNC (load the other deck's algorithm with its options), PLAY (only shown when a run is too costly to start on its own). |
| Crossfader and agreement meter | Back row, center                         | The blend of A and B, available to strips as the source MIX. Each deck's result is scaled 0 to 1 before blending, so different algorithms mix fairly. The agreement meter shows the rank correlation of the two runs.                                                                                                                     |
| Library browser and encoder    | Left wing                                | Folders: Attributes, Runs (every run made in this project, newest first), Algorithms (by family), Nodes, Presets. One encoder turns and presses.                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Channel strips                 | Center, 8 per bank, about 5 cm wide each | Slim strips: scribble strip (name, poke to select), meter, MUTE, SOLO, CUE, fader. An empty strip at the end makes a new one.                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Selected channel               | Right wing                               | The selected strip's full settings, at full size: INSERT, LOW, HIGH, SUBSET, ROUTE, INV, the assign buttons, readouts, and REMOVE when the strip is muted.                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Navigation block               | Front lip, left half                     | Jog platter, nudge strip, TILT knob, four hot cue pads.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Master section                 | Front lip, right half                    | Master display and meter (nodes and edges visible of the total), SEND, eight scene pads, UNDO, REDO, TALKBACK, BANK buttons, SAVE, SOUND, PLACE.                                                                                                                                                                                          |

The selected channel shows, top to bottom:

| Control                                          | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| INSERT slot                                      | The strip's source: an attribute, a run, MIX, a quick algorithm loaded straight in, a selection strip's membership, or (selection strips) its seed nodes.                        |
| LOW, HIGH                                        | The range of the source value the strip acts on. For a selection strip the source is hop distance from its seed nodes, so the knobs click in whole hops.                         |
| INV                                              | Act on everything outside LOW and HIGH instead of inside.                                                                                                                        |
| SUBSET (detented)                                | Which nodes the strip applies to: the whole graph, or any selection strip's members. A selection used as a subset counts its members whatever its own fader and mute say.        |
| ROUTE (detented)                                 | Send the strip to the master or to a group strip.                                                                                                                                |
| Assign buttons COLOR, SIZE, LABEL, OUTLINE, KEEP | Which property the strip drives; more than one may be lit. KEEP makes it a filter: its fader sets how far the dropped nodes fade (halfway leaves faint ghosts, full hides them). |
| REMOVE (muted strips only)                       | Delete the strip. You have already seen the graph without it, because it is muted.                                                                                               |

### The journey

1. **Enter.**
    - **What you do:** Put on the headset and open the Florentine families project.
    - **What you see:** The graph at chest height and the desk below it, placed from your height. Decks are empty, one empty strip waits in the center, and the master meter reads 15 of 15 nodes, 20 of 20 edges. Holding a fingertip just above any control shows its name.
    - **Controls:** none yet; hover for names. PLACE if you are seated at a table.

2. **Get oriented.**
    - **What you do:** Pinch the jog platter and turn it to spin the graph, poke it to stop, slide the nudge strip to bring the graph closer, tip it with TILT. Tap hot cue pad 1 to store this view.
    - **What you see:** The graph turning and stopping with the platter, coming closer, tipping; you stay still. Pad 1 lights.
    - **Controls:** jog platter, nudge strip, TILT, hot cue pad.

3. **Find the Medici.**
    - **What you do:** Either aim at the node labeled Medici and pinch, or turn the browse encoder to Nodes, scroll to Medici and press the encoder.
    - **What you see:** Medici ghost-highlighted on the cue bus. Found through the browser, where you could not see it, the turntable eases round to bring Medici to the front; aimed at directly, the graph stays put. The master meter is unchanged: nothing has reached the graph yet.
    - **Controls:** aim and pinch, or browse encoder.

4. **Who they married into.**
    - **What you do:** Press SEND. A new strip appears, "Selection: Medici", already selected, with OUTLINE assigned and its fader up. In the selected channel, turn HIGH from 0 to 1 hop, then LOW to 1 to leave Medici itself out. Press LABEL.
    - **What you see:** The outline spreads from Medici to its six marriage partners, Acciaiuoli, Albizzi, Barbadori, Ridolfi, Salviati and Tornabuoni, with their names; the strip's meter goes 1, 7, 6. The cue bus is empty again.
    - **Controls:** SEND, HIGH and LOW knobs, LABEL.

5. **Who matters most (PageRank).**
    - **What you do:** Tap deck A's LOAD to arm it, turn the browse encoder to Algorithms, Centrality, PageRank, and press the encoder.
    - **What you see:** Deck A shows PageRank with its option knobs (damping 0.85, iterations). The run is cheap, so it starts at once, and the progress ring fills and closes. The ranked strip appears and the run is listed under Runs. Nothing on the graph changes, because no strip uses it yet.
    - **Controls:** LOAD, browse encoder turn and press.

6. **Read the result.**
    - **What you do:** Press deck A's CUE, then hover a fingertip along its ranked strip.
    - **What you see and hear:** The graph shows the PageRank sizes as ghosts, and you hear a quick falling run, one note per family. Under the fingertip each family lights in turn with its name and value: Medici 0.146, then Guadagni 0.098, Strozzi 0.088, down to Acciaiuoli at 0.031; the note steps down with the value. Medici sits clearly apart at the top. Press CUE again to take it off.
    - **Controls:** CUE, hover along the ranked strip.

7. **Color and size by PageRank.**
    - **What you do:** Poke the empty strip's name to select it, tap INSERT, and press the encoder on "PageRank (deck A)" at the top of Runs. Press COLOR and SIZE, then push the strip's fader up slowly.
    - **What you see and hear:** As the fader rises, every node eases from its plain style toward the PageRank colors and sizes; at the top it snaps to the full mapping. The meter reads 15. Press SOLO and sweep your aim ray (no pinch) across the graph: the pitch is high over Medici and drops over Acciaiuoli. Press SOLO again to release.
    - **Controls:** strip name, INSERT, browse encoder, COLOR, SIZE, fader, SOLO, aim sweep.

8. **Keep only strong families (filter).**
    - **What you do:** Make another strip from "PageRank (deck A)" the same way, press KEEP, push its fader to the top, and turn LOW up. Near the end, keep holding the knob and lift the hand off the desk to slow it, and land on 0.070 (or hold the readout and type it).
    - **What you see and hear:** Families drop out as the knob passes their value, each with one click, and the meter counts down. At 0.070 five families remain: Medici, Guadagni, Strozzi, Albizzi and Tornabuoni. Ridolfi, at 0.0696, just missed, which is why fine mode is there. Pulling the fader halfway down would leave the dropped families as faint ghosts instead of hiding them.
    - **Controls:** strip name, INSERT, KEEP, fader, LOW knob, fine mode.

9. **Write a note.**
    - **What you do:** Aim at Medici and pinch to put it on cue. The master display reads "Note to: Medici". Hold TALKBACK and say "Medici lead PageRank by half again over Guadagni." Without voice, tap TALKBACK and type.
    - **What you see:** The text appears on the master display as you speak, then pins to Medici as a note. With nothing on cue the note goes to the project; with a strip or deck cued last, it goes to that strip or run.
    - **Controls:** aim and pinch, TALKBACK.

10. **Undo a mistake.**
    - **What you do:** Reaching for the color strip's fader, you pinch the filter strip's LOW knob instead and drag it up to 0.090 before you notice. Press UNDO.
    - **What you see:** The knob turns back to 0.070 by itself and blinks, so you can see which control moved; the meter climbs back to 5. REDO would put it back to 0.090. For a bigger retreat, tap a scene pad you stored earlier and the whole desk, every fader, knob and deck, returns to that moment; that recall is itself one UNDO step.
    - **Controls:** UNDO, REDO, scene pads.

11. **Save.**
    - **What you do:** Tap the empty scene pad 1 to store this mix as a scene, then press SAVE.
    - **What you see:** The pad lights and SAVE flashes once. The project holds the selection, style and filter strips in their order, the PageRank run with its options, the scene and the note. A muted strip is saved but changes nothing. The cue bus is never saved, exported or shown in a shared view.
    - **Controls:** scene pad tap, SAVE.

### Advanced journeys

- **Compound rule.** Strips with KEEP combine with AND: a node stays only if every KEEP strip passes it, and a node dropped by several fades as far as the strongest fader says. "Strong families that did not marry a Medici" is the PageRank filter strip plus a KEEP strip whose INSERT is the "Selection: Medici" strip's membership, with INV pressed: Medici, Guadagni and Strozzi remain (the Medici are not their own in-laws). For OR, load a Group from Presets into an empty strip, press its ANY button, and turn ROUTE on two KEEP strips to that group. The group strip's fader and mute then act on the whole rule at once.
- **Tuning algorithm options.** Deck knobs come from the algorithm's option schema: numbers are knobs, choices are detented knobs, on and off are buttons, and an attribute choice (such as edge weight) is a slot loaded from the browser. Turning damping reruns PageRank live when the run is cheap, and every strip using that run follows it, so you watch the colors and the filter move as you turn. When the element's cost estimate says the run is costly, the deck shows PLAY: knob changes wait, the deck says the result is stale, and PLAY runs it.
- **Comparing two runs.** Press SYNC on deck B to load PageRank with deck A's options, then turn B's damping to 0.5. Point the style strip's INSERT at MIX and sweep the crossfader: Medici and Guadagni hold their size, Salviati swells from tenth to fifth, and Albizzi and Strozzi trade places; the agreement meter shows how close the two rankings are overall. Load betweenness into B instead and Medici pulls even further ahead. Cue deck B alone to preview it as a ghost. Loading a new algorithm into a deck never takes a run away from a strip: the old run stays in Runs and the strips keep it. Only MIX follows whatever the decks hold.
- **Layers on subsets.** Turn a style strip's SUBSET knob to "Selection: Medici", and that layer paints only the Medici neighborhood. A quick algorithm loaded into a strip's INSERT with SUBSET set runs only over that subset, for example communities within the neighborhood.
- **Recipes.** A scene can be exported as a recipe, and a single strip can be stored in the browser's Presets folder. Loading a recipe on another graph rebuilds the strips and decks. Any source the new graph lacks shows as an unpatched INSERT slot with a blinking light, ready to be repointed with the encoder.
- **Joining tables.** Stays on the 2D page. Once a table is joined there, its columns appear under Attributes in the browser and can be loaded into any strip.

### How it scales

- **60+ algorithm catalog.** The browser lists algorithms by family folder, sortable by name or cost, with type-to-search from the desk keyboard. The decks never grow: whatever has more than four options pages its knobs, as DJ effect units do.
- **New object types.** A new kind of result becomes a new source in the browser; a new style property becomes a new assign button in the selected channel. Edges get their own bank of strips with the same anatomy. A layout can sit on a deck as well, and the crossfader then morphs node positions between two layouts.
- **Plugins.** A registered algorithm appears in its browser folder, and its options become deck knobs automatically from its declared schema. A plugin needs no desk code.
- **The limit.** Anything that is not a value over nodes or edges fits badly. Paths, matchings and flows reduce to membership, so their range knobs do little. Editing data (adding nodes, changing attributes) is not a mix and stays on the 2D page. Past about 16 live strips, banks hide what is shaping the graph; the master meter and SOLO help, but a heavy analysis outgrows one desk.

### On each device

| Device            | Desk                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Aiming at the graph                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Navigation                                        | Sound and voice                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Honest notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Meta Quest 3 / 3S | Hands: direct touch and pinch through WebXR hand input. Controllers: touch the tip to a control to press it, hold the trigger to hold a fader or knob, and a short haptic pulse per detent through the gamepad's haptic actuator. B or Y is UNDO. | Hand ray plus pinch, or controller ray plus trigger. The browser fires a select on every pinch, near or far; near beats far decides which one counts.                                                                                                      | Platter by hand, or thumbstick for jog and nudge. | Web Audio with spatial panning works in the Quest Browser. Speech recognition inside an immersive session is not dependable there and the system keyboard cannot be called up from one, so the desk keyboard is the expected TALKBACK path; test the Web Speech API rather than assume it. | No eye tracking reaches WebXR. Bare hands get no touch feedback, so PLACE onto a real table is worth teaching early.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Samsung Galaxy XR | Hands as on Quest through Chrome's WebXR hand input. The optional controllers are exposed as gamepads; check that haptics pass through.                                                                                                           | Hand ray plus pinch, or controller trigger.                                                                                                                                                                                                                | As on Quest.                                      | Web Audio works in Chrome; Chrome's speech recognition is the likely TALKBACK path, to be confirmed inside an immersive session. Desk keyboard otherwise.                                                                                                                                  | The system uses eye tracking, but pages are not given gaze.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Apple Vision Pro  | Hands: direct touch on the desk once the page is granted hand tracking (joint poses). Pinch-and-move holds a fader. No haptics, so detents click audibly.                                                                                         | Look at a node and pinch (WebXR transient pointer): the eye ray arrives only at the moment of the pinch, which is exactly enough to put a node on cue. Every pinch on the desk also produces a transient pointer; near beats far makes the page ignore it. | Platter and nudge strip by hand.                  | Spatial audio through Web Audio. Speech recognition inside an immersive session must be tested; the desk keyboard always works.                                                                                                                                                            | There is no aim ray to sweep while soloed (the eyes give no hover and visionOS has no hand rays), so on this device you hear a soloed strip by hovering along its deck's ranked strip, and each node you cue plays its note. Hover names appear only for a fingertip. PS VR2 Sense controllers are supported by visionOS; where Safari's WebXR exposes them, they map exactly as Quest controllers do, with haptics. Confirm on the current visionOS release. |

### Why it should work

- **Dynamic queries.** Sliders that update the view continuously (Ahlberg, Williamson and Shneiderman, 1992, and the FilmFinder that followed) let people explore faster and with fewer errors than forms with an apply step. The desk applies that result to everything: style, filter, selection and algorithm options.
- **Shipped VR precedent.** Tribe XR (a DJ trainer on Quest with virtual decks, jog wheels and a crossfader) and Electronauts show that people can work mid-air faders, knobs and jog wheels in a headset for long sessions.
- **One kind of control.** Every value is a fader or knob with the same feel and the same fine mode, so learning one strip teaches them all. Console users already move across 32-channel desks this way, because the strips repeat.
- **Selected channel.** Digital consoles keep the fader strips slim and show one selected channel's full settings in a single section at full size. That is what lets eight strips fit at a size bare fingers can hit.
- **Fine mode has a known precedent.** Moving the finger away from a slider to scrub more finely is how iOS media scrubbing has worked for years. Scaling motion for precision is also the idea behind PRISM (Frees and Kessler, 2005) in VR.
- **Real surfaces help.** Lindeman et al. (1999) found that a physical surface under virtual controls improves speed and accuracy. PLACE puts the desk on any table you have.
- **Scene recall and pre-fader cue.** Every digital mixing console ships scene recall and pre-fader listening; operators rely on them because they make bold changes safe and previews harmless.
- **Sonification of values.** Apple's Audio Graphs (VoiceOver, iOS 15 and later) ship pitch-mapped charts; The Sonification Handbook (Hermann, Hunt and Neuhoff, 2011) reports that pitch reads magnitude and trends well. Hearing a soloed strip adds a second channel for reading a result without looking away from the graph.
- **Comparison by blending.** Gleicher et al. (2011) name superposition as one of three basic comparison designs; a crossfader is superposition you control over time, and change blindness research suggests motion under your own hand makes differences pop out.

### What it would teach us

- Is "no apply step" calmer or more anxious? Do people trust a mix that is never committed, or do they keep storing scenes as a substitute for an apply button?
- Can a style layer, a filter, a selection and an algorithm option really share one control, or do users want different controls for different kinds of things?
- Does a filter as a fader, with dropped nodes fading by degrees, help people see what a threshold costs before they commit to it?
- Is crossfading two runs a better way to compare than side by side or a difference view? Do people find stable and unstable nodes faster?
- Does sonification add information in a working session, or is it a novelty that is switched off after a minute?
- How precise are mid-air faders with fine mode compared to typed values, and does a real table under the desk close the gap?
- Does keeping the hands off the graph (desk only) tire people less than grabbing the graph, or does it make the graph feel remote?
- Does selecting a strip and editing it in one section feel object-first, or do people reach for controls on the strip itself?

### Known risks

- **Mid-air faders with no touch.** Bare-hand faders overshoot. Mitigations: fine mode by lifting the hand, detents with a click sound and a controller pulse, PLACE onto a real table, a typed value by holding any readout.
- **Everything live means accidental changes.** A grab on the wrong control changes the graph at once, as in step 10. Mitigations: UNDO and REDO; the undone control blinks; a fader or knob only moves on a pinch or held trigger, and a button only presses on a push from the front, never on a brush; the desk can be moved out of the arm's path.
- **Costly runs fight the live model.** A slow algorithm cannot rerun on every knob turn. Mitigations: the deck shows PLAY when the cost estimate is high; knob changes then wait until PLAY, and the deck says the result is stale.
- **Strip overload.** With many strips it is hard to tell what shapes the graph. Mitigations: the master meter, the per-strip meters, SOLO, banks, and scribble strips that name each source.
- **Partial faders are hard to read.** A color at 40 percent is neither style. Mitigations: faders snap at 0 and 100 percent; the readout warns when a strip sits between them; saving with a strip in between asks once.
- **Sound in a shared office.** Mitigations: sound is never the only signal (meters and labels carry everything); the SOUND button; headphones recommended.
- **Console vocabulary is foreign to most analysts.** Mitigations: hover names on every control; plain labels (KEEP, not "gate"; UNDO, not "recall"); one strip anatomy everywhere; the first strip of a new project is made for you.
- **Desk below, graph above.** Looking down to the desk and up to the graph tires the neck. Mitigations: the desk sits just below the graph's lower edge rather than at the hips, it can be raised and pulled closer, the meters and master display can be read in peripheral view, and the hot cue pads and CUE bring the graph to you instead of you hunting through it. The graph moves, never the viewpoint, which keeps motion sickness low; spin speed is capped.
- **The element has to grow first.** A style layer's strength, a filter's fade, a source blended from two results and the agreement between two rankings are not things graphty-element offers today. They belong in graphty-element, where every consumer gets them; the desk must not compute them itself.

### Comparison row

| #   | Name        | One-line idea                                                                                                                                                                                      | Mode model                                                               | Navigation                                                                                       | Selection                                                                                          | Command surface                                                                                                                                                | Parameters                                                                                                            | Undo                                     |
| --- | ----------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| 21  | Mixing Desk | Console with a channel strip per style layer, filter and selection; nothing committed, every effect a live fader; two runs on two decks blended by a crossfader; cue and solo make a strip audible | live mix, no apply step; select a strip, edit it in the selected channel | jog platter turntable + nudge strip + tilt + hot cue pads; the graph moves, hands never touch it | aim and pinch onto a preview cue bus, SEND makes it a mixable selection strip; hops by range knobs | console: slim strips (fader, mute, solo, cue), selected-channel section (source, range, subset, route, assign), two decks, crossfader, library browser, master | faders and knobs moved along the desk, fine mode by lifting the hand, typed value on hold; crossfader blends two runs | UNDO / REDO for every change, scene pads |

## Prototype 22: Command Card

Real-time-strategy controls: box select in the air, control groups for selections and for camera views, a fixed grid of commands laid on your forearm, and orders queued to run in sequence.

### How it differs, layer by layer

| Layer                    | Command Card                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Differs from                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Mode model               | Select, then give an order from a fixed grid. Holding (or latching) the append modifier queues orders instead of running them; letting go runs the queue in order. Every verb sits at the same spot on the arm for every object type, so after a while you find it by feel, the way a strategy-game player finds grid hotkeys.                                | Context Menus (11) and Palette Hand (1) also select first, but their verbs move: a menu is built per object, a palette face is flipped. Gesture Grammar (3) learns positions as flick directions, not as places on the body. Nothing else plans a sequence of orders before running it. |
| Navigation               | Camera control groups: hold a camera cell to store the current view; tap it to see where it goes, tap twice to bring the graph to that view. Double-tapping a selection group frames that group. On controllers, the right thumbstick pushed past halfway slides the graph at a steady speed. Grab moves and turns the graph. You never move; the graph does. | Two-hand grab (1, 3), turntable stick (4), world in miniature (5), look-and-pull with radar (10). Here views are stored and recalled by number, like camera hotkeys.                                                                                                                    |
| Selection                | A 3D box pulled between two corners in the air; double-click a node to take every node of the same kind; four selection control groups stored and recalled on the back of the forearm (off thumbstick flicks on controllers). Append adds, subtract removes.                                                                                                  | Point and pinch (1, 2), lasso (3, 11), painting (4), tongs (6), lock-on (10), typed query (5). None stores numbered selections you recall by touch.                                                                                                                                     |
| Menus / command surfaces | A 3x5 grid laid along the inside of the off forearm. Its contents change with the selected object type, but each verb always sits in the same cell: Inspect at the wrist corner, Cancel last at the elbow corner. Algorithms live on a paged sub-grid behind Analyze, each at a fixed page and cell.                                                          | Hand-held palette with faces (1), belt and cartridge rack (4), fixed dock (5), pegboard (6), radial menus (3, 9, 17), menus at the object (11). Here the surface is your own arm and the layout never moves.                                                                            |
| Parameters               | Orders that need a target turn the pointer into a targeting cursor; you click the target. Orders that need a number turn the grid into a stepper: minus, value, plus across the middle row, and a slide along the length of the forearm to scrub.                                                                                                             | Sliders on the palette (1), spoken numbers (2), wrist twist (3), stick dial (4), typing (5), knobs (6, 8), finger counts (7).                                                                                                                                                           |
| Feedback                 | Your forearm gives real touch on every press; the pressed cell lights; controllers tick on press. A fingertip hovering over a cell previews what it would do to the graph. A queue strip above the arm shows pending orders in sequence, and the running one fills as it works.                                                                               | Visual-only buttons in the air (1, 10, 11); controller rumble alone (4).                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Undo                     | Before an order runs, pull its chip off the queue strip. After it runs, press the fixed Cancel last cell, which always cancels the newest order whatever state it is in; an undone order leaves a hollow chip you can drag back to redo it.                                                                                                                   | Palette button (1), "undo" word (2), thumb swipe (3), B button (4), receipts (6), wind time (9), undo counter (10).                                                                                                                                                                     |

**Setup:** solid colored background in full VR; the graph floats at chest height, 70-80 cm away. A column of object tiles (runs, style layers, filter steps, notes, recipes) stands just left of the graph, so objects that have no place in the graph can be clicked like anything else. Built for controllers (Quest, Galaxy XR, PS VR2 Sense controllers on Vision Pro where supported); hands work as the alternative and are the default on Vision Pro. The dominant hand points, clicks and presses cells; the off forearm carries the grid. Eyes are used only on Vision Pro, where a pinch aims where you look. Voice is optional and off until turned on: saying a cell's name presses it, and a note can be dictated. The off hand never pinches: on all three headsets, a pinch with the palm facing you is the system's own menu gesture.

### The control vocabulary

Dominant hand is the right hand here; everything mirrors for left-handed people.

| Input (hands / controllers)                                                                        | Meaning everywhere                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Point: hand ray / controller ray (Vision Pro: look, then pinch)                                    | Aim. Hovering names the thing under the pointer; nothing changes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Click: right index pinch / right trigger, on a thing                                               | Select it, replacing the selection. Click empty air: clear the selection (the graph itself is selected). Click while the targeting cursor shows: issue the waiting order at that target. With append or subtract on, a click on empty air does nothing.                                                     |
| Double-click: two quick pinches / two trigger pulls, on a node                                     | Select every node of the same kind (same type; or, when a style layer colors by a category, the same category). On anything other than a node it acts as one click.                                                                                                                                         |
| Pinch-drag / trigger-drag starting in empty air or on a node                                       | Pull a 3D box: the first corner sits on the pointer's line at the depth of the graph's center; move to the opposite corner, pushing the hand forward or back to set the box's depth. Release selects everything inside. Nodes are never dragged in this prototype.                                          |
| Pinch-drag / trigger-drag on a chip (a queue order or an object tile)                              | Move that chip: pull a queue order away to discard it; drag a hollow (undone) chip back onto the strip to redo it; drag a finished queue onto the object column to keep it as a recipe. Chips can be pinched directly or by ray.                                                                            |
| Grab: right fist / right grip, held                                                                | Hold the graph: move it, turn it, bring it closer.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Touch a cell on the inside of the off forearm: right index fingertip / tip of the right controller | Give the order in that cell, for the current selection. Contact, then lift, is a press; a touch that slides more than 1 cm is a scrub, not a press. A fingertip hovering within 3 cm lights the cell, names it on the status line and previews its effect on the graph.                                     |
| Turn the off palm up / palm down                                                                   | Palm up shows the command card on the inside of the forearm; palm down shows the group bar on the back of the forearm. Only the side facing you takes presses.                                                                                                                                              |
| Touch a group cell on the back of the off forearm                                                  | Selection group: tap recalls it, tap twice recalls and frames it. Camera group: tap shows an outline of where that view puts the graph, tap twice moves the graph there. Hold 0.6 s: store the current selection or view in it. While the targeting cursor shows, a tap on a group cell targets that group. |
| Off thumbstick flicked up, right, down, left (controllers only)                                    | Shortcut for selection groups 1-4, with the same tap, tap-twice and hold meanings.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Append: off fist held / off grip held, or the Append latch on the group bar                        | Append. Orders go onto the end of the queue instead of running; letting go (or tapping the latch off) runs the queue in order. Clicks, boxes and group recalls add to the selection instead of replacing it.                                                                                                |
| Subtract: the Subtract latch on the group bar / off trigger held                                   | Clicks, boxes and group recalls remove from the selection. On hands it is a latch only: tap it on, tap it off.                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Right thumbstick pushed past halfway (controllers only)                                            | Slide the graph in that direction at a steady speed set by how far it is pushed. No acceleration.                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Voice (optional, off by default): a cell's name, such as "Keep only" or "PageRank"                 | Press that cell of the card now showing, with the same preview on the status line. While a note is open: dictation.                                                                                                                                                                                         |

### Key structures

**The command card.** A 3 by 5 grid on the inside of the off forearm, rows from wrist to elbow, columns from the thumb side (A) to the little-finger side (C). The grid starts 4 cm up from the wrist crease (so your eyes land on the forearm, not the palm) and is scaled to the calibrated forearm, about 9 cm wide and 20 cm long; the outer columns land on the sides of the forearm. Each cell keeps its verb for every object type; a verb that does not apply to the selection is dark, and pressing it gives a dull tick and does nothing. A selection holds graph elements (nodes and edges together) or objects of one type; append-clicking an object of another type gives a dull tick and changes nothing.

| Cell              | Verb             | Nodes or edges                                                                                                        | Run (algorithm result)                   | Style layer                               | Filter step                   | Note                     | Recipe                  | Nothing selected (the graph)                              |
| ----------------- | ---------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ----------------------------- | ------------------------ | ----------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- |
| A1 (wrist corner) | Inspect          | attribute readout                                                                                                     | ranked readout                           | its rule                                  | what it removes, with a count | read it                  | list its steps          | summary: counts, attributes                               |
| B1                | Frame            | fit them in view                                                                                                      | fit the nodes it scored                  | fit what it paints                        | fit what it removes           | fit what it is pinned to | dark                    | fit the whole graph                                       |
| C1                | Grow             | add neighbors, one hop per press (edges: add endpoints)                                                               | dark                                     | dark                                      | dark                          | dark                     | dark                    | dark                                                      |
| A2                | Link to (target) | shortest path to the target node                                                                                      | dark                                     | limit it to the target selection or group | move it after the target step | pin it to the target     | replay it on the target | dark                                                      |
| B2                | Remove           | hide them (adds a filter step)                                                                                        | discard it (and the layers made from it) | delete it                                 | delete it                     | delete it                | delete it               | dark                                                      |
| C2                | Keep only        | keep only these (adds a filter step)                                                                                  | keep nodes above a value (stepper)       | keep only what it paints                  | dark                          | dark                     | dark                    | dark                                                      |
| A3                | Analyze          | open the algorithm sub-grid; run on these                                                                             | run it again with its current options    | dark                                      | dark                          | dark                     | dark                    | open the algorithm sub-grid                               |
| B3                | Compare (target) | overlap with the target group                                                                                         | compare with the target run              | dark                                      | dark                          | dark                     | dark                    | dark                                                      |
| C3                | Tune             | their numeric attributes                                                                                              | its options for the next run             | palette and range                         | its threshold                 | dark                     | the numbers inside it   | layout and project settings (name, voice, card placement) |
| A4                | Color by         | paint these one color (a layer on this subset)                                                                        | a layer colored by its values            | dark                                      | dark                          | dark                     | dark                    | attribute cells: color by one                             |
| B4                | Size by          | size these                                                                                                            | a layer sized by its values              | dark                                      | dark                          | dark                     | dark                    | attribute cells: size by one                              |
| C4                | Label            | show their labels                                                                                                     | show values as labels                    | dark                                      | dark                          | dark                     | dark                    | all labels on or off                                      |
| A5                | Note             | write a note on them                                                                                                  | write a note on it                       | write a note on it                        | write a note on it            | edit it                  | write a note on it      | write a note on the graph                                 |
| B5                | Save             | save the project                                                                                                      | same                                     | same                                      | same                          | same                     | same                    | same                                                      |
| C5 (elbow corner) | Cancel last      | cancel the newest order: waiting for a target or number, abandon it; running, stop it; queued, drop it; done, undo it | same                                     | same                                      | same                          | same                     | same                    | same                                                      |

**Preview before change.** Hovering over a cell that changes the graph shows the change faintly: Remove outlines what would be hidden or deleted (and names any layers that depend on a run), Keep only fades what would go, Color by and Size by tint and scale faintly. The status line names it with a count, for example "Remove: hide 3 nodes". Lifting the finger away without contact changes nothing.

**How orders and the selection fit together.** Every order acts on the selection as it stands when that order runs. Grow is the only order that changes the selection; orders that make an object (a run, a layer, a filter step, a note) add a tile to the column and leave the selection alone. Values chosen on the stepper while queueing are kept with the chip. Storing into a group or camera cell counts as an order, so Cancel last restores what the cell held before. Cancel last is never queued: pressed while append is on, it drops the newest queued chip.

**Cards that replace the command card for a moment.** The elbow corner is always Cancel last; on these cards it backs out of the card.

| Card             | When it shows                | Layout                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ---------------- | ---------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Analyze sub-grid | after Analyze                | Rows 1-4 hold 12 algorithms per page; A5 previous page, B5 next page, C5 back. Pages in fixed order: 1 centrality (PageRank at A1), 2 community, 3 paths, 4 structure, 5 similarity and link prediction, 6 flow and the rest, then plugin pages. An algorithm never changes page or cell. |
| Stepper          | an order needs a number      | Every cell dims except row 3 and C5: A3 minus, B3 the value, C3 plus. To scrub, touch B3 and slide toward the elbow to raise or toward the wrist to lower; the whole forearm length is the track. Lifting ends the scrub without accepting; a plain tap on B3 accepts.                    |
| Option list      | after Tune                   | Rows 1-4 list the object's options, one per cell; press a number option to open the stepper, a name option to open the keypad.                                                                                                                                                            |
| Keypad           | after Note, or a name option | Rows 1-4 are a phone keypad (multi-tap letters; * deletes, # is a space); A5 finishes; C5 abandons. Dictation, where the browser offers it, is faster; the keypad is the fallback for short text.                                                                                         |

**The group bar.** On the back of the off forearm, seen when the palm turns down. Three columns, the same 3 by 4 cm cells as the card.

| Row               | A (thumb side)    | B                 | C (little-finger side) |
| ----------------- | ----------------- | ----------------- | ---------------------- |
| 1, near the wrist | selection group 1 | selection group 2 | selection group 3      |
| 2                 | selection group 4 | Append latch      | Subtract latch         |
| 3                 | camera view 1     | camera view 2     | camera view 3          |
| 4, near the elbow | --                | --                | Cancel last            |

Selection group cells show their counts; a lit latch shows "append" or "subtract" on the strip too.
